# Consideration of funding proposals – Addendum XIV

## Funding proposal package for FP272

### Summary

The addendum contains the following seven parts:

1. A funding proposal titled "Protecting Infrastructure and assets at risk from Glacial Lake Outburst Floods (GLOFs) using remote sensing techniques focusing on glacial river basins affected by the sudden development of debris flow" submitted by the National Development Authority.
2. Institutional and social reporting disbursement.
3. Independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment, recommendations, and decisions.
4. Independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment, recommendations, and decisions.
5. Independent Technical Advisory Panel's assessment, recommendations, and decisions.

---

This document is a supplementary addendum to the Green Climate Fund Board Meeting held on 2 June 2023 in Bonn, Germany. The present addendum refers to Addendum XIV.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.1

## A. Implementation and Compliance

### A.10. Complementarity and Additionality
Yes: Yes (No)

The National Executive Entity for this project is the Government of Nepal, represented by the Ministry of Forests and Soil Conservation (MoFSC) and the Department of National Parks and Wildlife Conservation (DNPWC), which are responsible for implementing the project.

### A.20. Executing Entity
Yes: Yes (No)

The Host Country will receive a loan through the International Finance Corporation (IFC) and other implementing partners, with all key activities being implemented by the Government of Nepal (GoN). The Department of National Parks and Wildlife Conservation (DNPWC), Ministry of Forests and Environment, and the Department of Urban Development and Building Construction (DOUDCBC) will implement the project.

### A.30. Executive Summary
The project activities will be implemented through the GoN and other implementing partners, with all key activities being implemented by the Government of Nepal (GoN). The Department of National Parks and Wildlife Conservation (DNPWC), Ministry of Forests and Environment, and the Department of Urban Development and Building Construction (DOUDCBC) will implement the project.

1. **Nepal is a least developed country (LDC) identified by the United Nations as having a low income economy and weak human assets. The country has a high level of poverty, low life expectancy, and high infant mortality rates.** The country also faces significant challenges in terms of environmental degradation, deforestation, and loss of biodiversity. These challenges are exacerbated by climate change, which is leading to increased frequency and intensity of extreme weather events.

2. **The project will support the Government of Nepal's efforts to reduce greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation.** The project will also support the Government of Nepal's efforts to conserve and enhance carbon stocks in forests and other ecosystems. The project will be implemented through a combination of community-based approaches, market-based mechanisms, and policy reforms.

3. **Since the 1970s, Nepal has been working towards reducing its greenhouse gas emissions.** The country has implemented a number of policies and measures to reduce its greenhouse gas emissions, including the adoption of renewable energy sources, the promotion of energy efficiency, and the implementation of climate change adaptation measures. The project will build on these efforts and support the Government of Nepal's efforts to achieve its commitments under the Paris Agreement.

## Conclusion
The project activities will be implemented through the GoN and other implementing partners, with all key activities being implemented by the Government of Nepal (GoN). The Department of National Parks and Wildlife Conservation (DNPWC), Ministry of Forests and Environment, and the Department of Urban Development and Building Construction (DOUDCBC) will implement the project.

## A

### Community Marginalized groups including indigenous peoples' and women's "rights" - ensuring transparent (global) governance and social inclusion to help reduce inequality and promote sustainable development.

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a new funding mechanism designed to support projects, programmes, policies, and other activities in developing countries aimed at mitigating and adapting to climate change. The GCF was established in 2010 as part of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). It aims to deliver equal amounts of funding for mitigation and adaptation, with a focus on supporting vulnerable communities.

When the Government of Nepal GMU (Government of Nepal) acknowledges the need to promote investment in private sector-led climate change initiatives, the GCF can play an important role. The GCF provides funding for projects that are designed to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance resilience to the adverse effects of climate change. This includes support for renewable energy, energy efficiency, and sustainable transport.

### Appropriate use of GCF funds is being examined alongside USD40 million in loans to finance the implementation of GLOF (Glacial Lake Outburst Flood) risk reduction projects. The GCF will also provide funding for research into the impacts of climate change on the health of glaciers and the risks associated with GLOFs.

The GCF will provide funding for a range of activities, including:

- Support for renewable energy projects
- Funding for energy efficiency measures
- Support for sustainable transport initiatives
- Funding for adaptation measures to reduce the risks associated with climate change

The GCF will also provide funding for research into the impacts of climate change on the health of glaciers and the risks associated with GLOFs. This includes support for studies into the causes of glacial melting, the impacts of climate change on glacier health, and the development of early warning systems to reduce the risks associated with GLOFs.

### The compensation of interventions designed to reduce flood risks associated with glacial lakes acts over time to enhance ecosystem services and promote sustainable development.

For example, the GCF can provide funding for projects that:

- Reduce the risk of flooding by stabilizing glacial lakes
- Improve water management practices in areas affected by climate change
- Support the development of early warning systems to reduce the risks associated with GLOFs

The GCF can also provide funding for research into the impacts of climate change on glacier health and the risks associated with GLOFs. ## PROJECT/PROGRAMME INFORMATION

### Climate context (page, 10/50 words, approximately 2 pages)

**Glacier**

Glacier is located in Southern Asia, with a total area of 147.6 km² and a population of 25 million people. The glacier is one of the largest in the region and has been retreating rapidly over the past few decades. The retreat is due to climate change, which has led to higher temperatures and increased melting rates. This has resulted in the formation of glacial lakes, which are a significant hazard to the local population.

**Nepal**

Nepal is a least developed country (LDC) located as the 28th poorest nation globally and the second poorest in South Asia. The country is highly vulnerable to climate change, with over 70% of its population living below the poverty line. The country is also highly dependent on agriculture, which is vulnerable to climate change.

## A "GLOF" event when Thane 3, a large iceberg, broke off from the floating ice shelf of the Amery Ice Shelf in East Antarctica in March 2015.

Historically, the Amery Ice Shelf has been relatively stable with only minor calving events. However, recent studies have shown that the Amery Ice Shelf is now experiencing a significant increase in surface melt due to warm air temperatures and increased solar radiation. This has led to an acceleration of ice loss from the shelf.

The impact of GLOFs on downstream communities can be severe. In some cases, entire villages have been destroyed by the sudden influx of water. The economic costs of GLOF events can also be significant, with damage to infrastructure, agriculture, and fisheries. In addition, GLOFs can have a devastating effect on the environment, causing erosion and sedimentation in river valleys.

Damage caused by GLOFs is often exacerbated by human activities such as deforestation, mining, and construction. These activities can increase the risk of GLOF events by altering the natural drainage patterns of rivers and streams.

A total of 35 GLOF events have been recorded in Bhutan since 1967 and the country has been working to mitigate their impact through a variety of measures. One such measure is the construction of early warning systems that can detect the onset of a GLOF event and alert downstream communities to take appropriate action.

![Image](image_url)

## Figure 2: Damage attributable from a GLOF

Damage caused by GLOFs in Bhutan has been significant, with an estimated cost of US$10 million in 2017 alone. The country has been working to mitigate the impact of GLOFs through a variety of measures, including the construction of early warning systems and the implementation of land use policies that aim to reduce the risk of GLOF events.

![Image](image_url)

## Figure 4: Damage attributable from a GLOF

## B

12. In addition to GLOF, Nepal is vulnerable to floods resulting from extreme events, which cause similar types of damage and loss in the mountains as well as in the southern plains. The country has experienced several large-scale disasters including landslides, flash floods, and glacial lake outburst floods (GLOFs) in recent years. These events have caused extensive loss of life and property, disruption of livelihoods, and displacement of people. The government is taking steps to reduce vulnerability through improved disaster risk management, early warning systems, and climate change adaptation.

### Climate resilience

Climate change impacts on glaciers and GLOF:
- Climate change impacts on glaciers and GLOFs are most evident in response to rising temperatures and changes in precipitation patterns. Glacier retreat is a major indicator of climate change.
- The increase in the frequency and intensity of extreme weather events, such as heatwaves, heavy rainfall, and droughts, has also been observed.

14. As a result, the surface area and volume of a glacier can decrease at both high altitudes, creating a heightened risk of GLOF. Failure to manage glacial lakes can lead to catastrophic consequences. A 2013 study found that the number of glacial lakes in Nepal increased from 19 in 1977 to 46 in 2011. The volume of these lakes has also increased, with some lakes showing a rapid increase in recent years.

15. In addition to glacier retreat, other climate change-related factors contribute to the increased risk of GLOF:
- Changes in precipitation patterns and temperature can lead to an increase in the frequency and intensity of extreme weather events.
- Increased snowmelt and rainfall can lead to an increase in the likelihood of a GLOF event.

16. In recent years, the surface area and volume of glacial lakes have increased significantly. Much of the melting is attributed to rising temperatures and changes in precipitation patterns. The government has been working to reduce the risk of GLOFs by:
  - Implementing early warning systems for glacier lakes.
  - Conducting regular monitoring and assessment of glacial lake levels.
  - Developing and implementing plans to manage water resources effectively.

18. In recent decades, there has been a significant increase in the frequency and intensity of extreme weather events, including floods, landslides, and droughts. This has led to an increased risk of GLOFs and other climate-related disasters. The government is working to improve its capacity to respond to these events and to reduce their impact on communities.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.1 | PAGE 18

## IMPACT OF GCF's and potential for debt reduction

### Key benefits of GCF's and potential for debt reduction

GCF's can be used to finance climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries, including renewable energy, energy efficiency, and sustainable transport. These projects can help reduce greenhouse gas emissions, improve air quality, and promote sustainable development.

GCF's can also provide financing for climate change adaptation projects, such as flood defenses, early warning systems, and climate-resilient infrastructure. These projects can help protect communities from the impacts of climate change and build resilience to future shocks.

GCF's can be used to finance a wide range of projects, including renewable energy, energy efficiency, sustainable transport, and climate change adaptation. GCF's can also provide financing for technology transfer and capacity building, which can help developing countries access and deploy clean technologies.

### Complementarity with existing products, programmes and activities

GCF's can be used to complement existing climate finance initiatives, such as the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and the Adaptation Fund. GCF's can also be used to support national climate change strategies and action plans, which can help align climate finance with national priorities.

GCF's can be used to support a wide range of projects, including renewable energy, energy efficiency, sustainable transport, and climate change adaptation. GCF's can also provide financing for technology transfer and capacity building, which can help developing countries access and deploy clean technologies.

### Partnerships and collaboration

Partnerships are key to delivering on the promise of GCF's. GCF's can be used to support partnerships between governments, private sector entities, and civil society organizations. These partnerships can help leverage resources, share knowledge and expertise, and promote innovation.

GCF's can also be used to support regional and international cooperation, which can help build capacity and promote the sharing of best practices. GCF's can also be used to support the development of climate change policies and regulations, which can help create an enabling environment for climate action.

### Conclusion

GCF's offer a powerful tool for financing climate change mitigation and adaptation projects in developing countries. GCF's can provide financing for a wide range of projects, including renewable energy, energy efficiency, sustainable transport, and climate change adaptation. GCF's can also provide financing for technology transfer and capacity building, which can help developing countries access and deploy clean technologies.

GCF's can be used to complement existing climate finance initiatives, support national climate change strategies and action plans, and promote partnerships and collaboration. GCF's offer a powerful tool for promoting sustainable development and building resilience to the impacts of climate change.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal v.1.0 - Page 11

## B

### Background

The GCF is a funding mechanism established to support developing countries in achieving their climate change mitigation and adaptation objectives. It was adopted in Durban in 2011, with reference to other Annex I - Developing Country Engagement Report, while also providing guidance on the operationalization of the GCF.

#### Barriers to the preferred solution

There are several barriers that prevent developing countries from being able to access funding from the Green Climate Fund (GCF). These include:

- **Limited capacity**: Many developing countries lack the technical and financial resources needed to prepare and submit proposals to the GCF.
- **Lack of awareness**: There is limited knowledge among potential beneficiaries about the GCF's mandate, processes, and requirements.
- **Complex accreditation process**: The accreditation process for implementing entities is complex and time-consuming, which can deter potential applicants.

### Barrier 1 - Limited capacity to prepare and submit proposals

There are gaps in National Designated Authorities (NDAs) and accredited entities' capacities to effectively engage with the GCF. These include:

- **Limited technical expertise**: Many NDAs and accredited entities lack the technical expertise needed to develop high-quality proposals.
- **Lack of financial resources**: The cost of preparing and submitting proposals can be prohibitive for many developing countries.

### Barrier 2 - Limited understanding of GCF processes

There is a gap in understanding how the GCF operates, which limits the ability of NDAs and accredited entities to effectively engage with the Fund. This includes:

- **Limited knowledge of GCF policies**: Many NDAs and accredited entities are not familiar with the GCF's policies and procedures.
- **Lack of clarity on GCF processes**: The GCF's processes can be complex and confusing, which can deter potential applicants.

### Barrier 3 - Limited resources for long-term climate risk reduction in developing countries

The GCF is a key mechanism for supporting long-term climate risk reduction in developing countries. However, there are barriers to effective engagement with the Fund, including:

- **Limited understanding of GCF policies**: Many NDAs and accredited entities are not familiar with the GCF's policies and procedures.
- **Lack of clarity on GCF processes**: The GCF's processes can be complex and confusing, which can deter potential applicants.

### Barrier 4 - Limited resources for long-term climate risk reduction in developing countries

The GCF is a key mechanism for supporting long-term climate risk reduction in developing countries. However, there are barriers to effective engagement with the Fund, including:

- **Limited understanding of GCF policies**: Many NDAs and accredited entities are not familiar with the GCF's policies and procedures.
- **Lack of clarity on GCF processes**: The GCF's processes can be complex and confusing, which can deter potential applicants.

### Barrier 5 - Limited resources for long-term climate risk reduction in developing countries

The GCF is a key mechanism for supporting long-term climate risk reduction in developing countries. However, there are barriers to effective engagement with the Fund, including:

- **Limited understanding of GCF policies**: Many NDAs and accredited entities are not familiar with the GCF's policies and procedures.
- **Lack of clarity on GCF processes**: The GCF's processes can be complex and confusing, which can deter potential applicants.

## A.1 Pre-construction preparation (see attachment 3, approx. 5 months)

### Project description

The proposed project is a sub-national the loss and damage of people and their assets by reducing the risk of climate change impacts in the area of the Great Lake Drought Region (GLDR). This will be achieved through the implementation of the project activities that are designed to reduce the vulnerability of communities to drought. The project will be implemented by the Government of [Country] with support from the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and executed by the Ministry of Environment, Natural Resources and Scientific Research (MENRS).

The project aims to enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities in the GLDR through targeted training and awareness raising. The proposed project of project implementation will be carried out over a period of 5 years.

### Project objectives

- **Objective 1:** To increase the capacity of local governments to manage drought risk.
- **Objective 2:** To improve the livelihoods of vulnerable communities in the GLDR.
- **Objective 3:** To enhance the resilience of ecosystems and biodiversity in the region.

### Project activities

The project will be implemented through a series of activities that include:

1. **Activity 1:** Conducting vulnerability assessments to identify areas at risk from drought.
2. **Activity 2:** Developing and implementing early warning systems for drought.
3. **Activity 3:** Providing training and capacity building for local governments and communities on drought management.
4. **Activity 4:** Establishing community-based disaster risk management (CBDRM) committees to coordinate response efforts.

### Outputs

- **Output 1:** Vulnerability assessments completed in [Country].
- **Output 2:** Early warning systems established in the GLDR.
- **Output 3:** Training programs conducted for local governments and communities on drought management.
- **Output 4:** CBDRM committees established in all affected areas.

### Budget

The project budget is $X million, with funding from the GCF. This includes costs for:

- **Component 1:** Vulnerability assessments ($X).
- **Component 2:** Early warning systems ($X).
- **Component 3:** Training programs ($X).
- **Component 4:** CBDRM committees ($X).

### Implementation and technical strategy

The project will be implemented through a series of activities that include:

- **Activity 1:** Conducting vulnerability assessments.
- **Activity 2:** Developing early warning systems.
- **Activity 3:** Providing training programs for local governments and communities on drought management.
- **Activity 4:** Establishing CBDRM committees.

### Monitoring and evaluation

The project will be monitored and evaluated through a series of activities that include:

- **Activity 1:** Conducting vulnerability assessments.
- **Activity 2:** Developing early warning systems.
- **Activity 3:** Providing training programs for local governments and communities on drought management.
- **Activity 4:** Establishing CBDRM committees.

### Coordination with other projects

The project will be coordinated with the following projects:

- **Project X:** A national-level project to enhance climate resilience in [Country].
- **Project Y:** A regional-level project to improve early warning systems for drought.

These projects are not only related but also complement each other, ensuring a comprehensive approach to addressing the risks and impacts of drought.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V.1.3.1

## Figure 8: Locations of each of the larger global sites, including one on another and two smaller districts.

### Following the selection of the larger sites, an extensive analysis based on country modeling as well as international networks and green growth indicators was undertaken to identify potential smaller sites. This process included:

- **Identifying** the most suitable locations for smaller sites.
- **Conducting** a comprehensive analysis of the environmental impact and economic benefits of each site.
- **Evaluating** the feasibility of implementing Eco-District solutions in these areas.

### As a result, three additional sites were identified as suitable for implementation:

1. **Eco-District 1**: Located near the coast, this site has significant potential for renewable energy generation and sustainable tourism development.
2. **Eco-District 2**: Situated inland, this site is ideal for agricultural innovation and eco-tourism.
3. **Eco-District 3**: Positioned in a densely populated urban area, this site focuses on urban sustainability and green infrastructure.

### The selection process also highlighted the importance of local engagement and community involvement in the success of these projects. Furthermore, the Eco-District initiative aims to:

- **Reduce** the environmental impact of GLF sites.
- **Promote** sustainable development practices.
- **Enhance** the quality of life for residents.

### The implementation of Eco-Districts will involve close collaboration with local governments and communities. Key initiatives include:

- **Green Land**: Afforestation efforts in urban areas to improve air quality and reduce heat island effects.
- **Blue Land**: Restoration of coastal ecosystems to enhance biodiversity and protect against climate change impacts.
- **Grey Land**: Sustainable urban planning and infrastructure development to support green growth.

### These initiatives will be supported by comprehensive monitoring and evaluation frameworks to ensure the long-term success and sustainability of the Eco-District projects. The Green Climate Fund will continue to work closely with local governments, communities, and international partners to achieve these goals.

### 83
It is emphasized that the direct contribution of sea grass communities and mangrove forests in the carbon cycle is often overlooked. The potential for long-term carbon storage in these ecosystems has been demonstrated through various studies. The capacity to develop additional benefits from these ecosystems, such as fisheries enhancement and coastal protection, should be considered. Similarly, the importance of wetland services provided through Day 1.

### Potential activities
The following activities are proposed:

- **Restoration of degraded sea grass communities** (e.g., through planting of seeds or seedlings)
- **Protection of existing sea grass communities**
- **Establishment and enforcement of protected areas for sea grass communities**

### Sub-activities

## B

### 89
Executing Entity (EE) (for UNDP arrangements) is accountable to UNDP for managing the project, including the monitoring and evaluation of project activities achieved against objectives and for the reporting on progress towards results. The EE will be accountable to the IA for the use of funds and will report directly to the IA. All disbursements to the EE (Executing Agency) are conditional upon compliance with UNDP’s Financial Regulations and Rules.

### 90
**Accountable Entity:**
- The Ministry of Finance (MoF), through the Department of Energy and Mineral Resources (DEMR), is accountable for the overall implementation of the project.
- The Department of Energy and Mineral Resources (DEMR) under Ministry of Energy, Water Resources and Irrigation (MoEWR&I) will be the Executing Entity (EE).
- The Ministry of Finance (MoF), which will play an intermediary role in disbursing funds to the EE and Responsible Entities.

### 91
As part of the project, there will also be a need for achieving project objectives and for the achievement of UNDP results. The EE will be accountable to UNDP for managing the project and for the reporting on progress towards results. The EE is also responsible for the delivery of the project. UNDP will also provide oversight through UNDP Country Office in Nepal, the IA, and the DEMR.

### 92
In addition, the Government/National Project has requested UNDP to provide administrative services for this project.
- UNDP will provide administrative services for the project.
- UNDP will be responsible for the overall management of the project.
- UNDP will also ensure that all financial and procurement procedures are followed.

### 93
**National Implementing Entity:**
- The National Implementing Entity (NIE) is accountable to the EE for managing the project at the national level.
- The NIE is responsible for implementing the project activities and achieving the objectives of the project.

### 94
**Accountable Entity:**
- The Ministry of Finance (MoF), through the Department of Energy and Mineral Resources (DEMR), is accountable for the overall implementation of the project.
- The Department of Energy and Mineral Resources (DEMR) under Ministry of Energy, Water Resources and Irrigation (MoEWR&I) will be the Executing Entity (EE).
- The Ministry of Finance (MoF), which will play an intermediary role in disbursing funds to the EE and Responsible Entities.

### 95
The implementation will engage UNDP support various aspects to the country counterparts involved based on partner countries’ needs. This includes technical assistance, capacity building, and procurement services.
- Technical assistance is provided by UNDP and IA.
- Capacity building is provided by UNDP and IA.
- Procurement services are provided by UNDP and IA.

### 96
**Responsible Entities:**
- Responsible entities will be government and local authorities in MidHillHydropower in its role as an Executing Entity. As such, they will also enter into agreements with the MidHillHydropower specifically for the implementation of the project.

# Project Governance Arrangements

## Project Steering Committee

- **Secretariat**: Provides administrative support to the Project Board (UQ/UP and UQ/UC).
- **Project Board (UQ/UP and UQ/UC)**: Responsible for overall project governance, including strategic direction, resource allocation, and approval of major decisions.

### Implementation Partner UP

#### Project Management Office (PMO)

- **Responsibilities**: Provides project management support to the Project Board.
- **Key Activities**:
  - Develops and maintains the project plan.
  - Monitors progress against the plan.
  - Reports on project performance to the Project Board.

### Implementation Partner UQ

#### Project Management Office (PMO)

- **Responsibilities**: Provides project management support to the Project Board.
- **Key Activities**:
  - Develops and maintains the project plan.
  - Monitors progress against the plan.
  - Reports on project performance to the Project Board.

### Responsible Parties

#### UQ/UP

- **Responsibilities**: Implements the project as per the approved plan.
- **Key Activities**:
  - Delivers project outputs in accordance with the project plan.
  - Ensures compliance with project governance arrangements and standards.

#### UQ/UC

- **Responsibilities**: Provides technical expertise and support to the project.
- **Key Activities**:
  - Supports the implementation of the project by providing technical advice and guidance.
  - Ensures that project outputs meet required quality standards.

## Pre-Project Steering Committee

The pre-project steering committee will be established by the Project Board (PB), which will be set up by the MUEWRLDM, the project sponsor. The committee will include representatives from key stakeholder groups and will provide strategic direction to the project. It will also ensure that the project is aligned with the overall objectives of the organization.

### Roles and Responsibilities

- **Project Board (PB)**: Overall governance and oversight of the project.
  - Ensures alignment with organizational objectives.
  - Approves major decisions and changes to the project plan.
  - Monitors progress and ensures delivery of project outputs.

- **Implementation Partner UP**: Delivers the project as per the approved plan.
  - Develops and maintains the project plan.
  - Monitors progress against the plan.
  - Reports on project performance to the Project Board.

- **Implementation Partner UQ**: Provides technical support and expertise.
  - Supports implementation by providing technical advice.
  - Ensures compliance with standards and quality requirements.

### Key Activities

1. **Executive Sponsor**:
   - Provides strategic direction and ensures alignment with organizational objectives.
   - Champions the project within the organization.

2. **Project Manager**:
   - Manages day-to-day activities of the project.
   - Ensures delivery of project outputs in accordance with the plan.

3. **Technical Lead**:
   - Provides technical expertise to ensure that project outputs meet required standards.
   - Supports implementation by providing technical advice and guidance.

4. **Stakeholder Manager**:
   - Manages stakeholder relationships and ensures their needs are met.
   - Facilitates communication between the project team and stakeholders.

5. **Project Team**:
   - Delivers project outputs as per the approved plan.
   - Works closely with the Project Manager to ensure successful delivery of the project.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.1 PAGE 8

- Input Assistance (IA) performs the quality assurance role and reports the IA and Project Management Unit (PMU) to the Executive Director. The PMU is responsible for the overall management of the project, including implementation, monitoring and evaluation. The PMU can delegate any of its functions to the National Designated Authority (NDA). The NDA is the focal point in the country for all matters relating to the GCF. The NDA will be supported by a Project Management Office (PMO) and headed by a Project Manager.

## Implementation and Operational Arrangements

### 10.1

The PMU shall be led by a Project Director, who reports directly to the Executive Director. The PMU shall have a Project Management Officer (PMO), who is also responsible for the overall management of the project. The PMO will work closely with the NDA and other stakeholders in the government agencies.

### 10.2

The Project Manager shall be appointed by the Executive Director, subject to the approval of the Board. The Project Manager shall have a minimum of five years' experience in project management and shall have a degree in a relevant field. The Project Manager shall be responsible for the overall implementation of the project.

### 10.3

The PMU shall be supported by the Project Coordination Office (PCO), which will have the following responsibilities:

- Provide technical assistance to the NDA and other stakeholders.
- Facilitate the preparation of project proposals, including the identification of potential projects.
- Monitor the progress of approved projects and ensure that they are implemented in accordance with the approved plans and budgets.
- Coordinate with the NDA and other stakeholders to ensure that the project is implemented effectively.

### 10.4

The PCO shall be headed by a Project Coordinator, who will report directly to the Executive Director. The Project Coordinator shall have a minimum of five years' experience in project management and shall have a degree in a relevant field. The Project Coordinator shall be responsible for the overall coordination of the project.

### 10.5

The PCO shall be supported by a team of technical experts, who will provide technical assistance to the NDA and other stakeholders. The technical experts shall have a minimum of three years' experience in their respective fields and shall have a degree in a relevant field.

## Financial Arrangements

### 10.6

The financial arrangements for the project shall be governed by the GCF Board, a Funded Activity Agreement (FAA) with the signed between the NDA and the GCF, and the approved budget for the project. The FAA will include the following:

- A description of the project and its objectives.
- A detailed work plan for the implementation of the project.
- A detailed budget for the implementation of the project.

## 8.1 Justification for GCF Funding Proposal

### 8.1.1 Rationale

Negotiate a one-off loan from the Green Climate Fund (GCF) to finance the construction of the world's largest single climate change mitigation project, which will reduce emissions by over 50 million tons per year. Example: The construction of a large-scale solar power plant in the United States.

### 8.1.2 Background

The Government of Nepal has submitted a proposal to the GCF for funding to finance the construction of a large-scale solar power plant in the country. The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Energy, Water Resources and Irrigation (MoEWR) with technical assistance from the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP).

### 8.1.3 Funding Requirements

The Government of Nepal has requested $50 million to finance the construction of a large-scale solar power plant in the country. The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Energy, Water Resources and Irrigation (MoEWR) with technical assistance from the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP).

### 8.1.4 Justification

The Government of Nepal has submitted a proposal to the GCF for funding to finance the construction of a large-scale solar power plant in the country. The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Energy, Water Resources and Irrigation (MoEWR) with technical assistance from the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP).

### 8.1.5 Additional Information

The Government of Nepal has submitted a proposal to the GCF for funding to finance the construction of a large-scale solar power plant in the country. The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Energy, Water Resources and Irrigation (MoEWR) with technical assistance from the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP).

In the 2021/2022 UNDP Human Development Report, Nepal is ranked out of 189 countries on the Human Development Index (HDI). The country has a low HDI score of 0.576 and ranks 144th in the world. The report also indicates that Nepal's life expectancy at birth is 70 years.

### 110

Reports of goods and services accounted for 84% of Nepal's total GDP of USD 29 billion in 2019. These imports include food, clothing, machinery, electronics, vehicles, and other consumer goods. The country also exports goods such as carpets, textiles, garments, leather products, and handicrafts. The export sector is a significant contributor to the economy, accounting for approximately 8% of GDP.

### T01 addresses the need of countries, particularly SLOCs, for rapid and reliable input of information and integrated communication tools to support their climate change adaptation and mitigation efforts.

The project proposes to enhance the primary role of GLOF's through the provision of relevant information and tools that will enable them to effectively manage the risks associated with GLOFs. The project will also provide a platform for knowledge sharing and best practice exchange among stakeholders. The project will be implemented in collaboration with the Government of Nepal, the Department of Hydrology and Meteorology (DHM), and other relevant organizations.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 PAGE 8

## B

For private sector investment in climate adaptation risk reduction, City may utilize GLOF risk reduction funds to support projects that reduce climate risks and enhance resilience. The City will develop a climate adaptation plan that includes the following elements: (a) identification of priority areas for climate adaptation; (b) assessment of current and future climate impacts on these priority areas; (c) development of strategies to address identified vulnerabilities; and (d) implementation of projects to reduce risks in these priority areas. The City will also develop a plan to engage the private sector in the implementation of these projects.

### The mechanisms for establishing and executing these public-private partnerships will be developed under the following principles:

1. **Identifying opportunities**: The City will identify potential partners and projects that are consistent with the goals of GLOF risk reduction.
2. **Developing partnerships**: The City will develop partnerships with private sector entities to implement projects that reduce climate risks in priority areas.
3. **Funding and financing**: The City will explore funding and financing mechanisms to support the implementation of these projects, including grants, loans, and other forms of financial assistance.

### Capacity development and knowledge sharing for climate-responsive planning

1.1 The project will enhance capacity building and knowledge sharing among local governments in the country of origin (e.g., City of Kathmandu) through the following activities:

- **Training and workshops**: Conduct training sessions and workshops to build the capacity of local government officials, community leaders, and other stakeholders to implement climate-responsive planning.
- **Knowledge exchange**: Facilitate knowledge exchange between local governments in the country of origin and other countries that have experience in climate adaptation planning.

1.2 The project will promote knowledge sharing discovered during implementation by facilitating collaboration between GLOF project partners and other relevant organizations, including:

- **Networking events**: Organize networking events to bring together GLOF project partners and other stakeholders to share experiences and best practices.
- **Publications and reports**: Publish reports and articles on the findings of the project and its impact on climate-responsive planning.

1.3 The project will promote knowledge sharing discovered during implementation by facilitating collaboration between GLOF project partners and other relevant organizations, including:

- **Networking events**: Organize networking events to bring together GLOF project partners and other stakeholders to share experiences and best practices.
- **Publications and reports**: Publish reports and articles on the findings of the project and its impact on climate-responsive planning.

### The project implementation plan

The project is consistent with the objectives listed in the NAPA/Least Developed Countries Fund (LDCF) project development plan (GLOF/2020/001/GCP). What follows is a summary of the key components of the project implementation plan:

- **Project activities**: The project will include a range of activities to support climate adaptation planning, including training, workshops, and knowledge exchange.
- **Timeline**: The project will be implemented over a period of three years, with specific milestones for each activity.
- **Budget**: The project budget will be developed based on the estimated costs of the activities and the resources available from GLOF risk reduction funds.

# Operation and Maintenance Beyond the Project Lifetime

## Regional Operations and Maintenance (ROM) of the tea leaf processing infrastructure

### EVC installations and other equipment

The Regional Operations and Maintenance (ROM) of the tea leaf processing infrastructure, EVC installations and other equipment will be carried out by the District Local Government (DLG). The DLG will be responsible for the operation and maintenance of the tea leaf processing infrastructure. This includes the following:

- **Operation and Maintenance (O&M)**: The O&M will be carried out by the DLG in accordance with the guidelines provided in Year 5, and continue to T12 years until end of warranty period.
- **Maintenance**: The DLG will be responsible for the maintenance of the tea leaf processing infrastructure. This includes regular inspection, repair, and replacement of parts as needed.
- **Training**: The DLG will provide training to the local community on how to operate and maintain the equipment.

### Additional Responsibilities

In addition to the O&M, ROM will also be responsible for the following:

- **Regular Inspections**: Conduct regular inspections of the tea leaf processing infrastructure to ensure it is functioning properly.
- **Repairs**: Carry out repairs as needed to keep the infrastructure in good working condition.
- **Training**: Provide ongoing training to the local community on how to operate and maintain the equipment.

### Funding

The Government of Nepal has given top priority to promote ecotourism, which are included in the National Adaptation Programme of Action (NAPA). The government is committed to providing financial support for the implementation of this project. The project will be funded through the Green Climate Fund (GCF), with additional funds from other sources as needed.

### Implementation

The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Forests and Soil Conservation (MoFSC), in collaboration with local communities, NGOs, and other stakeholders. The project will work with government agencies to ensure that the project is implemented effectively and efficiently.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 PAGE 4

## C. Capacity building and technology development/trainer needs - 2 weeks, approximately 6.5 pages

### C.1 Introduction

**Objective:** To provide an overview of the capacity-building needs for developing countries to implement innovative climate change mitigation technologies.

### C.2 Technology transfer

The proposed project is designed to enhance technology transfer by implementing innovative climate change mitigation technologies in developing countries (DCs) and least developed countries (LDCs). These technologies include:

- **Renewable energy:** Solar, wind, hydro, geothermal
- **Energy efficiency:** Lighting, appliances, buildings
- **Carbon capture and storage (CCS):** Pre-combustion, post-combustion, and oxy-fuel

The development plan aims to be implemented over the project period. The capacity-building activities will focus on local training centers for technology transfer. This includes:

- Training of trainers in advanced technologies
- Development of local expertise in climate change mitigation technologies
- Establishment of regional training centers for technology transfer

### C.3 Capacity-building needs

The capacity-building and technology development needs include the following:

1. **Development methods:** Development of national and local needs will be determined through a comprehensive assessment.
2. **Technology transfer:** Development of methods and standards for technology transfer.
3. **Training programs:** Establishment of training programs in advanced technologies.
4. **Policy support:** Development of policies to support capacity-building activities.
5. **Institutional strengthening:** Strengthening of institutions to support capacity-building activities.

The project will also focus on the development of climate-informed decision-making and management systems, including:

- **Data management:** Development of data management systems for climate change mitigation technologies.
- **Monitoring and evaluation:** Establishment of monitoring and evaluation systems for technology transfer.
- **Capacity-building:** Development of capacity-building programs for technology transfer.

### C.4 Conclusion

The proposed project aims to enhance the capacity of developing countries to implement innovative climate change mitigation technologies. The project will focus on the development of national and local needs, training programs, policy support, institutional strengthening, data management, monitoring and evaluation, and capacity-building programs.

The project will also support the development of a monitoring framework for climate-smart agriculture (CSA) and climate-resilient value chains. # EXPECTED PERFORMANCE AGAINST INVESTMENT CRITERIA

The performance criteria for the proposal are as follows:

- D1: Impact expected (over 50 women, approximately 1 year)
- D2: Job creation and livelihood development by providing quality assured seeds of the kinema plants from GLOF, setting up micro-enterprises and enabling community-led GLOF awareness for the promotion of sustainable agriculture.
- D3: Improved food security and nutrition through increased income and enhanced household food security and nutrition.

## Fund and finance details

- The project will be funded by various sources including Government of Nepal (GoN), bilateral donors, and other international donors contributing to GCF's first adaptation window (ADAP).
- The GoN has already committed $1.5 million for the project.
- The project will also receive co-financing from the World Bank and the Green Climate Fund (GCF).

## Implementation details

To utilize the primary output of the GLOF, the seeds will be loaned at the low price value (Thulo Lopen, Lumbini, Lumbini) to the farmers. Lending the seeds will be done through the local community-based organizations and the local government bodies. The seeds will be provided to the farmers at a subsidized rate. The project will also provide training on sustainable agriculture practices and climate-smart farming techniques. The project will also provide support for the establishment of micro-enterprises and co-operatives, which will enable the women to sell their products in the market.

## Project impact

This project will directly benefit 583,000 people (396 women and 187 men; 17% of the population) in the project area. The project will also provide indirect benefits to the local communities by creating jobs and increasing income levels. The project will also contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), particularly SDG 1 (No Poverty), SDG 2 (Zero Hunger), SDG 3 (Good Health and Well-being), SDG 5 (Gender Equality), SDG 8 (Decent Work and Economic Growth), SDG 9 (Industry, Innovation, and Infrastructure), and SDG 13 (Climate Action).

## Project management

The project will be implemented by the Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry (MOAF) in collaboration with the local government bodies. The project will also involve the private sector and non-governmental organizations to ensure the sustainability of the project.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1: PAGE 4

The proposed project intends on addressing the rate of a GCF by the fact that at most global states in Europe, especially the United Kingdom, have been reported to be suffering from climate change. Studies have shown that climate change has led to an increase in temperature and rainfall patterns which have affected the agricultural sector. This proposal aims to address the issue of climate change by providing funding for projects that aim to mitigate its effects.

## Objectives

1. To reduce greenhouse gas emissions.
2. To promote sustainable agriculture.
3. To enhance climate resilience.
4. To support renewable energy initiatives.
5. To foster international cooperation on climate change.

### Strategies

- Implementing renewable energy sources such as solar and wind power.
- Promoting energy efficiency in buildings and industries.
- Encouraging the adoption of sustainable farming practices.
- Supporting research and development in climate technology.
- Facilitating public awareness campaigns on climate change.

## Implementation Plan

The project will be implemented over a period of five years with the following milestones:

1. **Year 1**: Conduct a comprehensive assessment of current greenhouse gas emissions and identify key areas for intervention.
2. **Year 2**: Develop and implement policies and regulations to promote renewable energy and energy efficiency.
3. **Year 3**: Launch public awareness campaigns and provide training on sustainable farming practices.
4. **Year 4**: Support research and development in climate technology and provide funding for innovative projects.
5. **Year 5**: Evaluate the impact of the project and identify areas for further improvement.

## Budget

The total budget for the project is $10 million, with the following allocations:

- Renewable energy initiatives: $3 million
- Energy efficiency programs: $2 million
- Sustainable farming practices: $2.5 million
- Research and development: $1.5 million
- Public awareness campaigns: $1 million

## Monitoring and Evaluation

The project will be monitored and evaluated on an annual basis to ensure that the objectives are being met. The evaluation will include:

- Assessing the reduction in greenhouse gas emissions.
- Evaluating the impact of renewable energy initiatives.
- Measuring the adoption of sustainable farming practices.
- Reviewing the outcomes of research and development projects.
- Gauging the effectiveness of public awareness campaigns.

## Conclusion

The proposed project aims to address the issue of climate change by providing funding for initiatives that promote renewable energy, energy efficiency, and sustainable farming practices. By implementing these strategies, the project seeks to reduce greenhouse gas emissions, enhance climate resilience, and support international cooperation on climate change.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V.1.5 PAGE 8

## Executive Summary

### Accelerated investment in the climate sector

The impact of SLOFIs and other climate-related financing has been significant. The sector has seen an increase in investments, with a notable rise from $30 billion in 2016 to $47 billion in 2019. This growth is attributed to increased awareness and commitment to addressing climate change. The sector has also witnessed an influx of private capital, which has been crucial in driving innovation and scaling up solutions.

### Vulnerability of beneficiary groups

The vulnerability of certain groups to climate change is well-documented. It is estimated that 65% of the population will be affected by climate change by 2030. This includes low-income households, women, children, and elderly individuals who are particularly vulnerable to extreme weather events such as floods, droughts, and heatwaves.

### Institutional capacity needs

Institutional capacity is a critical factor in the effectiveness of SLOFIs. The sector has seen an increase in investments, with a notable rise from $30 billion in 2016 to $47 billion in 2019. This growth is attributed to increased awareness and commitment to addressing climate change. The sector has also witnessed an influx of private capital, which has been crucial in driving innovation and scaling up solutions.

## Next Steps

### Next Horizon Development Index (NHDI) value for 2019 was 0.652, which ranks as 142nd out of 183 countries surveyed. The NHDI measures the capacity of a country to achieve its development goals. The NHDI is calculated based on several indicators including:

- **Economic Development Index (EDI)**: Measures economic performance and growth.
- **Social Development Index (SDI)**: Measures social well-being and quality of life.
- **Environmental Sustainability Index (ESI)**: Measures environmental sustainability.

### Green Climate Fund (GCF) readiness program

The GCF Readiness Program aims to enhance the capacity of developing countries to access and effectively use climate finance. The program provides technical assistance, capacity-building activities, and funding to support national climate change planning processes. The program has been successful in helping countries develop their own climate change strategies and plans.

### The Paris Agreement

The Paris Agreement is a landmark international treaty that aims to limit global warming to well below 2 degrees Celsius above pre-industrial levels and pursue efforts to limit the increase to 1.5 degrees Celsius. The agreement was adopted by 196 parties at COP21 in Paris, France, on December 12, 2015.

### Climate Change Performance Index (CCPI)

The CCPI is an annual assessment of the climate protection performance of 61 countries and the EU. It evaluates countries' greenhouse gas emissions, renewable energy use, and climate policy. The index is published by Germanwatch, NewClimate Institute, and Climate Action Network International.

### Additional Resources

- [Green Climate Fund](https://www.greenclimate.fund)
- [Next Horizon Development Index (NHDI)](https://nhdi.org)
- [Paris Agreement](https://unfccc.int/process-and-meetings/the-paris-agreement)
- [Climate Change Performance Index (CCPI)](https://germanwatch.org/en/ccpi)

## 108. National Third Nation Contributions (TNC) to the NFCCC (2025-2030)

The NFCCC (2025-2030) presents an overview on the progress made towards achieving the goals of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). The GCF will continue to support the implementation of these projects. The GCF will also provide funding for the development of new technologies that can be used in the fight against climate change.

## 109. Second Nationally Determined Contributions (NDC) (2030-2040)

New studies suggest that GFC funding is essential to achieve the goals set out in the Paris Agreement. The GCF will continue to support countries in their efforts to implement these projects and provide funding for new technologies.

## 110. Green Climate Fund (GCF) (2035-2045)

The GCF will continue to fund projects that aim to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and help vulnerable communities adapt to the impacts of climate change. The GCF will also support the development of new technologies that can be used in the fight against climate change.

## 111. National Third Nation Contributions (TNC) on Risk Management (2035-2045)

The GCF will continue to fund projects that aim to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and help vulnerable communities adapt to the impacts of climate change. The GCF will also support the development of new technologies that can be used in the fight against climate change.

## 112. The Sendai Framework for Disaster Risk Reduction (2015-2025)

The GCF will continue to fund projects that aim to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and help vulnerable communities adapt to the impacts of climate change. The GCF will also support the development of new technologies that can be used in the fight against climate change.

## 113. The Environmental Protection Act (2015)

The Environmental Protection Act aims to protect and enhance the environment by preventing or minimizing pollution and ensuring sustainable use of natural resources. The act also provides for the establishment of an environmental protection agency, which is responsible for enforcing the provisions of the act.

## D

Underpinning of critical legacy sites and environmentally sensitive areas and UX benefits for environmental stewardship and climate change mitigation. The GCF will support the development of a Green Climate Fund (GCF) to provide long-term, low-cost financing for projects that contribute significantly to addressing climate change. The GCF will be designed to support projects that are consistent with the goals of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals.

### The Framework Plan (2019/2020 - 2035/2040)

The proposed plan aligns with the priorities laid out in the DERA's Framework Plan. It is designed to support the development of a Green Climate Fund that will provide long-term, low-cost financing for projects that contribute significantly to addressing climate change. The GCF will be designed to support projects that are consistent with the goals of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals.

### Stakeholder Engagement

The plan has been developed in a collaborative and inclusive manner with communities from across the region. It is intended to provide a framework for the development of a Green Climate Fund that will provide long-term, low-cost financing for projects that contribute significantly to addressing climate change. The GCF will be designed to support projects that are consistent with the goals of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals.

- **The Ministry of Energy Water Resources and Major Projects (MEWRP)**: The MEWRP is the Executive Entity of the Government of the Republic of the Union of Myanmar. It is responsible for the development, implementation, and management of energy, water resources, and major projects in the country.
- **The Department of Energy (DOE)**: The DOE is a department within the Ministry of Energy Water Resources and Major Projects. It is responsible for the development, implementation, and management of energy policies and programs in the country.
- **The Department of Environmental Conservation (DEC)**: The DEC is a department within the Ministry of Energy Water Resources and Major Projects. It is responsible for the development, implementation, and management of environmental conservation policies and programs in the country.
- **The Department of Climate Change (DCC)**: The DCC is a department within the Ministry of Energy Water Resources and Major Projects. It is responsible for the development, implementation, and management of climate change policies and programs in the country.

### Next Steps

The GCF will be established as a separate entity from the Government of the Republic of the Union of Myanmar. It will be governed by a Board of Directors that will include representatives from the government, private sector, and civil society. The GCF will be capitalized through contributions from the Government of the Republic of the Union of Myanmar, international organizations, and other stakeholders.

The GCF will provide long-term, low-cost financing for projects that contribute significantly to addressing climate change. It will support projects that are consistent with the goals of the Paris Agreement and the Sustainable Development Goals. The GCF will also support projects that are designed to promote sustainable development and economic growth in the country.

- Green guarantees
- Green insurance

# Green Climate Fund Proposal V1.3.1 PAGE 49

## Project Title: Urban Heat Reduction through Nature-Based Solutions for Urban Heat Island Mitigation and Climate Resilience

### Project Description:
At present, GHG emissions from urban areas account for more than 70% of global CO2 emissions. This project aims to reduce these emissions by implementing nature-based solutions (NBS) that can mitigate the urban heat island effect and improve climate resilience.

### Goals and Objectives:
- Reduce urban temperatures by at least 1°C through NBS implementation.
- Increase green spaces in urban areas by 20% within five years.
- Improve air quality and reduce energy consumption by 15%.

### Implementation Strategy:
There is an urgent need to implement these solutions as soon as possible. The project will focus on the following key activities:

1. **Green Infrastructure Development**: Implementing green roofs, walls, and urban forests to increase vegetation cover in cities.
2. **Urban Planning and Design**: Incorporating climate-responsive design principles into new developments and retrofitting existing buildings.
3. **Community Engagement**: Educating communities on the benefits of NBS and involving them in project planning and implementation.

### Project Timeline:
- **Year 1**: Conduct feasibility studies, engage stakeholders, and develop a detailed implementation plan.
- **Years 2-5**: Implement green infrastructure projects, monitor progress, and evaluate outcomes.

### Budget:
The total budget for the project is $10 million over five years. This includes costs for planning, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation.

## Project Title: Hydrological and Socio-Economic Solutions for Improved Water Management in Urban Areas

### Project Description:
This project aims to improve water management in urban areas by implementing hydrological and socio-economic solutions. The project will focus on reducing flood risk, improving water quality, and enhancing the resilience of urban communities to climate change.

### Goals and Objectives:
- Reduce flood risk by 30% through improved drainage systems.
- Improve water quality by 25% through better waste management practices.
- Enhance community resilience to climate change by 20%.

### Implementation Strategy:
The project will be implemented in two phases:

1. **Phase 1**: Conduct a comprehensive assessment of the urban area's hydrological and socio-economic conditions, including an analysis of flood risk, water quality, and community vulnerabilities.
2. **Phase 2**: Implement solutions identified during Phase 1, such as improving drainage systems, implementing green infrastructure for water management, and engaging communities in climate change adaptation efforts.

### Project Timeline:
- **Year 1**: Conduct assessments and develop a detailed implementation plan.
- **Years 2-5**: Implement solutions, monitor progress, and evaluate outcomes.

### Budget:
The total budget for the project is $8 million over five years. This includes costs for planning, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation.

   - Funding
   - Technical assistance

#### II. Annual Proposals

1. Number of annual proposals:
   - 1 proposal per year

2. Type of support requested in the proposal:
   - Funding
   - Technical assistance

### Project Implementation

#### I. Project Components

1. **Component 1: Integrated coastal zone management and climate change adaptation**
   - Objective: To enhance the resilience of coastal communities to climate change impacts.
   - Activities:
     - Development of integrated coastal zone management plans.
     - Implementation of nature-based solutions for coastal protection.

2. **Component 2: Renewable energy access**
   - Objective: To increase access to renewable energy in rural and urban areas.
   - Activities:
     - Installation of solar home systems.
     - Development of mini-grids powered by renewable energy sources.

3. **Component 3: Climate-smart agriculture**
   - Objective: To enhance the resilience of agricultural systems to climate change.
   - Activities:
     - Promotion of climate-smart farming practices.
     - Establishment of early warning systems for extreme weather events.

#### II. Project Timeline

1. Start date and duration:
   - Start date: January 2023
   - Duration: 5 years

2. Key milestones:
   - Completion of integrated coastal zone management plans by the end of year 1.
   - Installation of solar home systems in at least 1,000 households by the end of year 2.
   - Establishment of climate-smart farming practices in at least 5,000 hectares by the end of year 3.

### Project Monitoring and Evaluation

#### I. Monitoring Framework

1. Indicators:
   - Number of households with improved access to renewable energy.
   - Area of land under climate-smart agriculture practices.
   - Number of communities with enhanced resilience to climate change impacts.

2. Data sources:
   - Household surveys
   - Field visits
   - Remote sensing data

#### II. Evaluation Criteria

1. Environmental sustainability:
   - Reduction in greenhouse gas emissions.
   - Enhancement of ecosystem services.

2. Social and economic benefits:
   - Improvement in livelihoods.
   - Increase in access to modern energy services.

3. Gender equality:
   - Empowerment of women and girls.
   - Inclusion of gender-responsive approaches.

### Project Management

1. **Project Management Unit (PMU)**
   - Responsibilities:
     - Overall management of the project.
     - Coordination with stakeholders.
     - Monitoring and evaluation of project progress.

2. **Stakeholder Engagement**
   - Key stakeholders:
     - Government agencies
     - Local communities
     - Private sector entities

3. **Risk Management**
   - Identification of potential risks.
   - Development of mitigation strategies.

### Project Financing

1. **Funding Structure**
   - GCF funding: $20 million
   - Co-financing: $5 million (from national budget)

2. **Financial Projections**
   - Annual expenditure: $4 million per year for 5 years.
   - Total project cost: $20 million.

3. **Funding Arrangements**
   - Disbursement of funds based on achievement of milestones.
   - Regular reporting to GCF on project progress and financial performance.

### Project Closure

1. **Conditions for Closure**
   - Completion of all project activities.
   - Achievement of project objectives.
   - Submission of final evaluation report.

2. **Lessons Learned**
   - Documentation of best practices.
   - Identification of areas for improvement in future projects.

# Green Climate Fund Funding Proposal V1.0 Page 7

## E

### Need and Rationale

The rationale for the GCF funding proposal is based on the need to support developing countries in their efforts to mitigate and adapt to climate change.

### Objective

The objective of the GCF funding proposal is to provide financial resources to developing countries for mitigation and adaptation projects.

### Proposed Activities

- **Data acquisition and frequency of data collection**: Collect data on greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions and removals.
- **Standardization procedures for analysis and reporting**: Establish standardized procedures for analyzing and reporting GHG data.
- **Quality assurance/quality control (QA/QC)**: Implement QA/QC measures to ensure the accuracy and reliability of GHG data.
- **Capacity building**: Provide training and support to developing countries to enhance their capacity to manage and report GHG data.

### Expected Outcomes

The establishment of the GCF database will be important to:

- Support the implementation of the Paris Agreement.
- Enhance transparency and accountability in climate action.
- Improve monitoring, reporting, and verification (MRV) systems.
- Strengthen and enhance existing QA/QC procedures.
- Ensure consistency with other international databases.

### Monitoring and Evaluation

The proposed activities will be monitored and evaluated to ensure they are achieving the desired outcomes. This will include:

- Regular progress reports.
- Independent evaluations.
- Feedback from stakeholders.

### Use of GCF Resources

GCF resources will be used to support the following activities:

- Data collection and analysis.
- Standardization procedures.
- QA/QC measures.
- Capacity building for developing countries.

### Coordination with Other Funds

The proposed activities will be coordinated with other funds, such as the Global Environment Facility (GEF) and the Adaptation Fund (AF), to ensure consistency and avoid duplication of efforts. This coordination will include:

- Sharing information and data.
- Collaborating on capacity building initiatives.
- Aligning monitoring and evaluation processes.

### Additional Information

For inquiries and GCF's use of resources, please contact [Contact Information].

---

### Execution of Grant Agreements

GCF will execute grant agreements with accredited entities to implement projects funded by the GCF. These agreements will outline the terms and conditions for project implementation, including:

- Project objectives and activities.
- Budget and financial management.
- Monitoring and evaluation procedures.

### Accreditation of Entities

GCF will accredit entities that meet its standards for governance, transparency, and accountability. Accredited entities will be eligible to access GCF funding for projects that align with the GCF's priorities.

### Use of GCF Resources

GCF resources will be used to support projects that contribute to climate change mitigation and adaptation in developing countries. These projects may include:

- Renewable energy projects.
- Energy efficiency initiatives.
- Climate-smart agriculture.
- Early warning systems for climate-related disasters.

### Monitoring and Evaluation

GCF will monitor and evaluate the implementation of projects funded by GCF to ensure they are achieving their intended outcomes. This will include:

- Regular progress reports from accredited entities.
- Independent evaluations of project performance.
- Feedback from stakeholders, including communities affected by projects.

### Reporting Requirements

Accredited entities will be required to submit regular reports on the progress and impact of projects funded by GCF. These reports will include information on:

- Project activities and achievements.
- Financial expenditures.
- Environmental and social impacts.
- Lessons learned and best practices.

### Use of Information Generated

GCF will use the information generated through its funding proposals, grant agreements, and monitoring and evaluation processes to inform its policies and strategies for supporting climate action in developing countries. This information will be shared with other international organizations and stakeholders to enhance global efforts to address climate change.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 PAGE 79

## E

Below methods and tools will be standardised for GCF's implementation processes that include technical assistance and capacity building to support countries in their climate change planning, programming, and reporting.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.0 PAGE 8

## E

### Section 1: Introduction to GCF

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) was established in 2010 as a fund under the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). It aims to support projects and programs in developing countries that aim to mitigate or adapt to climate change.

### Section 2: Funding Mechanisms

GCF funding is provided through various mechanisms, including:

- **Funding Window**: Direct access entities and accredited national implementing entities can submit proposals for funding.
- **Readiness and Preparatory Support**: This supports countries in preparing their own projects and accessing GCF funds.
- **Private Sector Facility**: Encourages private sector investment in climate change mitigation and adaptation.

### Section 3: Project Cycle

The project cycle involves several stages:

1. **Concept Note**: Initial proposal outlining the project idea.
2. **Project Proposal**: Detailed plan submitted for approval.
3. **Appraisal**: Evaluation of the proposal by GCF.
4. **Approval**: Decision on funding made by the GCF Board.
5. **Implementation**: Project execution phase.
6. **Supervision and Monitoring**: Ongoing oversight to ensure project goals are met.

### Section 4: Key Documents

Key documents required for a successful application include:

- **Project Proposal Document (PPD)**: Detailed plan outlining the project's objectives, activities, budget, and expected outcomes.
- **Environmental and Social Management Plan (ESMP)**: Ensures compliance with environmental and social safeguards.

### Section 5: Funding Process

The funding process involves several steps:

1. **Concept Note Submission**: Initial proposal submission.
2. **Appraisal**: Evaluation by GCF.
3. **Approval**: Decision on funding made by the GCF Board.
4. **Contracting**: Formal agreement between GCF and implementing entity.
5. **Disbursement**: Release of funds for project implementation.

### Section 6: Compliance and Reporting

Compliance with environmental, social, and gender safeguards is mandatory. Regular reporting on progress and outcomes is required to ensure accountability and transparency.

### Section 7: Additional Resources

Additional resources and guidance documents are available on the GCF website to support applicants throughout the funding process.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.0 PAGE #1

## E

### Objective

Morogoro Region is the Kulikilo Kibosho and Sita in the Andesha District.

The region has a high level of poverty, low levels of education, and limited access to basic services such as water, sanitation, health care, and electricity. The region also faces significant environmental challenges, including deforestation, soil erosion, and climate change impacts.

### Proposed Activities

#### 1.1 Strengthen the technical and institutional capacity of local communities to implement climate-smart agriculture practices and renewable energy solutions.

- Training workshops and field visits for farmers on climate-smart agriculture techniques.
- Distribution of climate-resilient seeds and seedlings.
- Establishment of community-based early warning systems for weather-related disasters.
- Development of micro-irrigation systems to improve water use efficiency.

#### 1.2 Develop and implement a renewable energy strategy

- Conduct feasibility studies for small-scale renewable energy projects, such as solar and wind power.
- Provide technical assistance to local communities in the installation and maintenance of renewable energy systems.
- Facilitate access to financing for renewable energy projects through partnerships with financial institutions.

### 2.2 Develop and implement a climate change adaptation strategy

- Conduct vulnerability assessments to identify areas most at risk from climate change impacts.
- Develop and implement adaptation measures, such as water harvesting systems and climate-resilient crops.
- Provide technical assistance to local communities in the implementation of adaptation measures.

### 3.1 Strengthen institutional capacity for climate change mitigation and adaptation

- Training workshops and field visits for government officials on climate change mitigation and adaptation strategies.
- Development of policies and regulations to support climate change mitigation and adaptation efforts.
- Facilitate access to financing for climate change mitigation and adaptation projects through partnerships with international financial institutions.

### 4.1 Monitoring, Evaluation, and Reporting

- Establish a monitoring system to track progress towards the project's objectives.
- Conduct regular evaluations of the project's impact and effectiveness.
- Prepare and submit annual reports on the project's progress to the Green Climate Fund.

### 5.1 Training, Workshops, and Awareness Raising

- Conduct training workshops for local communities on climate change mitigation and adaptation strategies.
- Organize awareness-raising campaigns to promote climate-smart agriculture practices and renewable energy solutions.
- Facilitate partnerships with educational institutions to support long-term capacity building.

### 6.1 Community Engagement and Participation

- Establish community-based organizations to oversee the implementation of project activities.
- Conduct regular consultations with local communities to ensure their needs are addressed.
- Provide resources and support for community-led initiatives to promote climate change mitigation and adaptation efforts.

### 7.1 Gender Equality and Social Inclusion

- Ensure that women, youth, and marginalized groups have equal access to project benefits.
- Develop and implement gender-responsive strategies to address the specific needs of these groups.
- Conduct regular assessments to monitor progress towards gender equality and social inclusion objectives.

### 8.1 Financial Management

- Establish a robust financial management system to ensure efficient use of funds.
- Conduct regular audits to ensure transparency and accountability in financial management.
- Provide training for project staff on financial management best practices.

### 9.1 Environmental and Social Safeguards

- Develop and implement environmental and social safeguard policies to protect vulnerable groups and ecosystems.
- Conduct environmental impact assessments to identify potential risks and mitigation strategies.
- Establish a grievance mechanism to address any environmental or social concerns raised by project stakeholders.

### 10 Community-Based Monitoring Systems

- Establish community-based monitoring systems to track the progress of project activities.
- Provide training for community members on data collection and analysis techniques.
- Facilitate regular meetings with local communities to review project progress and address any concerns.
