# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.0 PAGE 8

## Section W: The Green Climate Fund and Proposal

### Objective
To provide an overview of the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and explain how it relates to this proposal.

### GCF Overview
The Green Climate Fund (GCF) is a global fund created to support the efforts of developing countries to respond to the challenge of climate change. It was established in 2010 as part of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). The GCF aims to promote a low-emission, climate-resilient development pathway by providing financial resources to developing countries.

### GCF Funding Mechanism
GCF funding is allocated through various channels, including direct access entities and accredited national designated authorities. Direct access entities can directly access GCF funds for projects that meet the fund's criteria. National designated authorities, on the other hand, act as intermediaries between the GCF and implementing entities in their countries.

### Proposal Alignment
This proposal aligns with the objectives of the GCF by focusing on climate change mitigation and adaptation initiatives. The proposed activities are designed to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and enhance resilience to the impacts of climate change.

## Energy Efficiency Requirements Management Structure

### Objective
To establish a comprehensive management structure for energy efficiency requirements in local government areas.

### Key Components
- **Policy Development**: Develop policies that mandate energy efficiency standards.
- **Compliance Monitoring**: Implement monitoring systems to ensure compliance with set standards.
- **Capacity Building**: Provide training and resources to local governments and businesses.
- **Public Awareness Campaigns**: Raise awareness about the importance of energy efficiency.

### Implementation Plan
1. **Policy Formulation**: Draft policies that align with national energy efficiency targets.
2. **Stakeholder Engagement**: Engage key stakeholders, including local governments, businesses, and communities.
3. **Monitoring Systems**: Develop and implement monitoring systems to track progress.
4. **Training Programs**: Conduct training sessions for local government officials and business owners.

### Funding
Funding will be sourced from a combination of GCF grants, national budget allocations, and private sector investments.

## Youth Groups and Women's Committees

### Objective
To empower youth groups and women's committees to participate in climate change mitigation and adaptation efforts.

### Key Activities
- **Capacity Building**: Provide training on climate change issues and sustainable practices.
- **Community Engagement**: Facilitate community meetings to discuss climate change impacts and solutions.
- **Project Implementation**: Support the implementation of small-scale climate resilience projects.
- **Advocacy**: Advocate for policies that support women and youth in climate action.

### Implementation Plan
1. **Needs Assessment**: Conduct assessments to identify specific needs and priorities.
2. **Training Workshops**: Organize workshops to build capacity among youth and women's groups.
3. **Project Support**: Provide resources and technical assistance for project implementation.
4. **Advocacy Campaigns**: Launch advocacy campaigns to influence policy decisions.

### Funding
Funding will be sourced from GCF grants, international development agencies, and local government budgets.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V1.0 PAGE E

## Objective

To develop and implement a comprehensive approach to the climate change challenges facing the country.

### Proposed Actions

- **Action 1:** Develop and implement National Adaptation Plans (NAPs) to enhance resilience to climate change impacts.
- **Action 2:** Integrate climate change considerations into national development plans, policies, and strategies.
- **Action 3:** Strengthen institutional capacity for climate change planning and management.

### Detailed Activities

#### Activity 1.1: National Adaptation Plans (NAPs)

- Formulate NAPs to be developed with technical assistance from international organizations.
- Integrate climate risk information into sectoral plans, policies, and programs.
- Conduct vulnerability assessments and develop adaptation measures for key sectors.

#### Activity 1.2: Integration of Climate Change Considerations

- Incorporate climate change considerations into national development plans.
- Develop and implement climate-smart agriculture practices to enhance food security.
- Integrate climate risk management into disaster risk reduction strategies.

#### Activity 1.3: Institutional Capacity Building

- Strengthen the capacity of key institutions to manage climate change initiatives.
- Provide training and technical support to government agencies responsible for climate change planning.
- Establish a national climate change information system to support decision-making.

### Implementation Strategy

The proposed actions will be implemented through a phased approach over a five-year period. The initial phase will focus on developing the necessary frameworks and policies, while subsequent phases will concentrate on implementation and monitoring.

### Monitoring and Evaluation

Progress will be monitored using specific indicators for each activity. Regular reports will be submitted to track achievements against set targets.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.3 PAGE 85

## E

### Collection, Every Transaction, Sell Price, Taxes (Section 6)

Every transaction will be recorded in a ledger. The ledger will be maintained by the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and hosted on the blockchain. The blockchain will ensure that all transactions are transparent and immutable. Each transaction will include the following information:

- **Transaction ID**: A unique identifier for each transaction.
- **Timestamp**: The date and time of the transaction.
- **Seller Information**: Details about the seller, including their name and address.
- **Buyer Information**: Details about the buyer, including their name and address.
- **Product Information**: Details about the product being sold, including its type and quantity.
- **Sale Price**: The agreed-upon price for the transaction.
- **Taxes**: Any applicable taxes or fees associated with the transaction.

### Planting 10% of Trees

1. **Planting 10%** of trees across various countries worldwide, such as:

   - **Country A**: 5 trees planted per hectare
   - **Country B**: 7 trees planted per hectare
   - **Country C**: 3 trees planted per hectare

2. **Total Trees Planted**: 150 trees in Country A, 210 trees in Country B, and 90 trees in Country C.

### Under Section 6.1: Environmental Risk Regulation (Eco-Oil)

Under Section 6.1, the Environmental Risk Regulation (Eco-Oil) will be implemented to ensure that all transactions are environmentally friendly. This regulation will include:

- **Environmental Impact Assessments**: Conducted for every transaction.
- **Carbon Footprint Tracking**: Monitored and reported annually.

### This Information

This information shall be prominently displayed on the website of the Green Climate Fund (GCF). The website will provide a user-friendly interface for users to access this information easily. The website will also include:

- **Contact Information**: Details on how to contact the GCF.
- **FAQ Section**: Answers to common questions about the program.

### 3.1 Independent Audits

Independent audits will be conducted annually to ensure compliance with the regulations outlined in this proposal. The audits will focus on:

- **Transaction Accuracy**: Ensuring all transactions are accurately recorded.
- **Environmental Compliance**: Verifying that environmental standards are met.
- **Financial Integrity**: Confirming the financial integrity of the GCF.

### 3.2 Reporting

Annual reports will be published detailing the findings of the independent audits and any corrective actions taken. The reports will include:

- **Audit Results**: Detailed results of each audit conducted.
- **Corrective Actions**: Steps taken to address any issues identified in the audits.
- **Environmental Impact**: Assessment of the environmental impact of the program.

### 3.3 Transparency

Transparency is a key component of this proposal. The GCF will ensure that all information related to transactions, audits, and environmental compliance is publicly available. This includes:

- **Transaction Ledger**: Publicly accessible on the blockchain.
- **Audit Reports**: Published annually on the GCF website.
- **Environmental Data**: Available for public scrutiny.

### 3.4 Compliance with International Standards

The GCF will comply with international standards and best practices for the verification of transactions, audits, and environmental compliance. This includes:

- **ISO Standards**: Adherence to ISO standards for environmental management systems.
- **Greenhouse Gas Protocol**: Use of the Greenhouse Gas Protocol for reporting greenhouse gas emissions.

### 3.5 Capacity Building

Capacity building will be undertaken for the implementation of this proposal. This includes training and support for:

- **Staff**: Training for GCF staff on transaction recording, auditing, and environmental compliance.
- **Partners**: Support for partner organizations in implementing environmental risk regulations.

### 3.6 Monitoring and Evaluation

Monitoring and evaluation will be conducted to assess the effectiveness of this proposal. This includes:

- **Key Performance Indicators (KPIs)**: Development of KPIs to measure success.
- **Regular Reviews**: Regular reviews to ensure ongoing compliance with environmental standards.

### 4.1 Implementation Plan

The implementation plan for this proposal is as follows:

- **Phase 1**: Development of the blockchain and ledger system.
- **Phase 2**: Initial transactions recorded on the blockchain.
- **Phase 3**: Environmental risk regulation implemented.
- **Phase 4**: Independent audits conducted annually.

### 4.2 Budget

The budget for this proposal is as follows:

| Item                          | Cost (USD) |
|-------------------------------|-----------|
| Blockchain Development       | 50,000    |
| Initial Transactions          | 10,000    |
| Environmental Risk Regulation | 30,000    |
| Independent Audits            | 20,000    |
| Capacity Building             | 15,000    |
| Monitoring and Evaluation     | 10,000    |
| **Total**                     | **145,000** |

### 4.3 Risk Management

Risk management will be an integral part of this proposal. This includes:

- **Identification**: Identifying potential risks.
- **Assessment**: Assessing the likelihood and impact of each risk.
- **Mitigation**: Developing strategies to mitigate identified risks.

### 5.1 Legal Framework

The legal framework for this proposal will include:

- **Contracts**: Contracts with partners and stakeholders.
- **Regulations**: Compliance with international regulations.
- **Dispute Resolution**: Mechanisms for resolving disputes.

### 5.2 Governance Structure

The governance structure for the GCF will include:

- **Board of Directors**: Responsible for overall governance.
- **Executive Committee**: Oversees day-to-day operations.
- **Advisory Board**: Provides expert advice on environmental and financial matters.

### 6.1 Conclusion

In conclusion, this proposal outlines a comprehensive approach to ensuring transparency, accountability, and environmental compliance in the Green Climate Fund (GCF). By implementing these measures, the GCF can effectively manage transactions, monitor environmental impact, and ensure financial integrity.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.1.3 | PAGE 8

Project Description: Activities under this sub-sector will be implemented through Integrated Natural Resource Management (INRM) approach to enhance ecosystem services and improve livelihoods of local communities. The project development of the INRM approach will be based on the principles of sustainable management of natural resources, biodiversity conservation, climate change adaptation and mitigation, and poverty reduction. The project will also promote the use of climate-smart agriculture practices, including agroforestry, integrated pest management, and water harvesting techniques. These activities will contribute to the achievement of the CBD's Aichi Biodiversity Targets, the UN Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), and the Paris Agreement on Climate Change.

## E.7. Monitoring, Reporting and Evaluation

The project will implement a monitoring and evaluation system that includes both quantitative and qualitative indicators. The system will be designed to track progress towards the project's objectives and outcomes, as well as to assess the impact of the project on local communities and the environment. The monitoring and evaluation system will be based on the following principles:

- Regular data collection and analysis
- Use of standardized tools and methods
- Participation of local communities in the monitoring process
- Timely reporting and dissemination of results

The project will also establish a system for reporting and evaluation to the Green Climate Fund (GCF) and the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC). This system will include regular reports, as well as an annual progress report and an end-of-project evaluation. The monitoring and evaluation system will be designed to ensure that the project is on track to achieve its objectives and to identify any areas where adjustments may be needed.

## E.8. Project Management Unit (PMU)

The PMU will be responsible for the overall management of the project, including planning, implementation, monitoring, and evaluation. The PMU will be composed of a team of experts in natural resource management, climate change adaptation and mitigation, and biodiversity conservation. The PMU will work closely with local communities, government agencies, and other stakeholders to ensure that the project is implemented effectively and efficiently.

The PMU will also be responsible for the day-to-day administration of the project, including financial management, procurement, and human resources management. The PMU will be based in the capital city of the country and will have a field office in each of the project's implementation sites.

## E.9. Financial Management

The project will have a separate budget for its implementation costs, which will be managed by the PMU. The budget will include funds for personnel costs, equipment, travel, and other expenses related to the implementation of the project. The financial management system will be based on the following principles:

- Transparency and accountability
- Regular financial reporting
- Compliance with relevant laws and regulations
- Effective use of resources

The PMU will also be responsible for the preparation of annual work plans and budgets, as well as for the monitoring of expenditures and the reporting of financial results to the GCF and other stakeholders.

## E.10. Risk Management

The project will include a risk management plan that identifies potential risks and outlines strategies for managing them. The risk management plan will be based on the following principles:

- Identification of potential risks
- Assessment of the likelihood and impact of each risk
- Development of strategies for mitigating or managing each risk
- Regular review and updating of the risk management plan

The PMU will be responsible for implementing the risk management plan and for monitoring and reporting on the status of any identified risks. The PMU will also be responsible for developing contingency plans in case any of the identified risks materialize.

## E.11. Gender Equality and Social Inclusion

The project will promote gender equality and social inclusion by ensuring that women, men, girls, and boys have equal access to project benefits and opportunities. The project will also promote the empowerment of vulnerable groups, including indigenous peoples, local communities, and people with disabilities.

The PMU will be responsible for implementing a gender equality and social inclusion plan that includes the following elements:

- Gender-responsive planning and budgeting
- Training and capacity building for women and vulnerable groups
- Monitoring and evaluation of gender equality and social inclusion outcomes
- Regular reporting on progress towards gender equality and social inclusion objectives

The PMU will also be responsible for ensuring that the project is implemented in a way that is sensitive to the needs and rights of all stakeholders, including women, men, girls, boys, indigenous peoples, local communities, and people with disabilities.

## E.12. Safeguards

The project will comply with the GCF's environmental and social safeguard policies and procedures. The PMU will be responsible for ensuring that the project is implemented in a way that minimizes any adverse impacts on the environment or on vulnerable groups.

The PMU will also be responsible for developing and implementing a safeguards management plan that includes the following elements:

- Identification of potential environmental and social impacts
- Assessment of the likelihood and severity of each impact
- Development of strategies for mitigating or managing each impact
- Regular monitoring and reporting on the status of any identified impacts

The PMU will also be responsible for ensuring that the project is implemented in compliance with relevant laws and regulations, including those related to environmental and social safeguards.

## E.13. Communication and Stakeholder Engagement

The project will include a communication and stakeholder engagement plan that outlines strategies for engaging with local communities, government agencies, and other stakeholders. The PMU will be responsible for implementing this plan and for ensuring that the project is implemented in a way that is transparent, inclusive, and responsive to the needs and concerns of all stakeholders.

The communication and stakeholder engagement plan will include the following elements:

- Development of a communication strategy
- Establishment of a stakeholder engagement process
- Regular reporting on project progress and achievements
- Organization of events and meetings to facilitate dialogue and collaboration

The PMU will also be responsible for ensuring that the project is implemented in a way that promotes transparency, accountability, and mutual respect among all stakeholders.

## E.14. Lessons Learned

The project will include a lessons learned plan that outlines strategies for capturing, documenting, and sharing knowledge and experience related to the implementation of the project. The PMU will be responsible for implementing this plan and for ensuring that the project is implemented in a way that promotes continuous learning and improvement.

The lessons learned plan will include the following elements:

- Identification of key lessons learned
- Documentation of these lessons in a format that is accessible and useful to others
- Dissemination of these lessons through various channels, including reports, presentations, and workshops
- Regular review and updating of the lessons learned plan

The PMU will also be responsible for ensuring that the project is implemented in a way that promotes knowledge sharing and collaboration among all stakeholders.

## E.15. Project Closure

The project will include a project closure plan that outlines strategies for completing all project activities, settling all financial obligations, and documenting all project results. The PMU will be responsible for implementing this plan and for ensuring that the project is completed in a way that is transparent, accountable, and consistent with GCF requirements.

The project closure plan will include the following elements:

- Completion of all project activities
- Settlement of all financial obligations
- Documentation of all project results
- Preparation of a final report

The PMU will also be responsible for ensuring that the project is closed in a way that promotes sustainability, accountability, and mutual respect among all stakeholders.

### RULES

A project that is **Red Rat** (project can achieve its intended outcomes by Green Climate Fund) is the most preferred and desirable way. Following are the evaluation criteria for determining the risk profile of a project:

- **Management experience:** The management experience is the evaluation recommendation along with an implementation plan for undertaking the required actions.
- **It is the responsibility of the Applicant to ensure that the project is designed by the UNCDF Country Office as well as the UNCDF Regional Technical Advisor.**

### TOR

The Terms of Reference (ToR) will be following the Four Evaluation Principles, which is a standard by UNDP at a global level. The ToR will include the following:

- **Evaluation:** This includes an evaluation report at the end of project implementation.
- **Monitoring:** This includes regular monitoring and reporting on the progress of the project.
- **Learning:** This includes lessons learned from the project implementation in the Legal Framework.
- **Accountability:** This includes accountability, good documentation, and transparency.

### RIS

The UNCDF Country Office will follow the risk-based approach for project evaluation in the UNCDF Country Office. The risk-based approach will include:

- **Risk Identification:** All evaluation reports including recommendations are based on risk identification.
- **Risk Assessment:** Risk assessment is done by the UNCDF Country Office.
- **Risk Mitigation:** Risk mitigation is done by the UNCDF Country Office.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V3 - PAGE N/A

## GCF POLICIES AND STANDARDS

### Environmental and Social Safeguards (ESS)

The Environment and Social Safeguards associated with this project have been evaluated in accordance with GCF’s Environmental and Social Safeguards Policy (ESSP). The ESSP is designed to ensure that projects funded by the GCF do not cause or exacerbate adverse environmental and social impacts. These safeguards are applied to all projects, including those managed by accredited entities.

### Environmental Management Framework (EMF)

The Environmental Management Framework (EMF) has been prepared to summarize the potential environmental and social risks associated with this project. The EMF is a key component of the GCF’s Environmental and Social Safeguards Policy. It provides an overview of the ESSP requirements, with the objective of ensuring that projects are managed in accordance with these safeguards.

### Environmental Impacts

Environmental impacts are primarily associated with the project itself to potential impacts associated with the construction activities and the operation phase of the project. These include:

- **Air Quality:** Potential increases in air pollution levels due to construction activities.
- **Water Quality:** Potential impacts on water quality from construction activities.
- **Noise:** Potential noise pollution during construction phases.

### Social Impacts

To address the potential damage caused by human rights, all construction activities will take place during the dry season. This will reduce the potential for a negative social impact on the technology, tool, and materials used in the project. The project will also include measures to mitigate any adverse impacts associated with the project’s implementation. These measures will be developed as part of the project’s ESMS.

### Gender Impacts

Gender impacts are minimal since women benefit from the project in the same way as men. There have been no gender-specific issues identified during the project development process. The project will continue to monitor and address any potential gender-related issues that may arise.

### Climate Change Adaptation and Mitigation

The project aims to reduce greenhouse gas emissions by 50% through energy-efficient lighting, ventilation, and waste management. This will help to minimize the impact of climate change on the environment. The project will also include measures to mitigate any adverse impacts associated with climate change.

### Biodiversity Impacts

Biodiversity impacts are minimal since the project does not involve any activities that could lead to habitat destruction or loss of biodiversity. The project will continue to monitor and address any potential biodiversity-related issues that may arise.

### Indigenous Peoples

The project has been designed in accordance with the principles associated with the protection of indigenous peoples’ rights. The project will also include measures to mitigate any adverse impacts associated with the project’s implementation. These measures will be developed as part of the project’s ESMS.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3 / PAGE No.

When a country accesses financing from the Green Climate Fund (GCF), it is expected to demonstrate that the support will enhance its capacity to implement climate change actions and contribute to sustainable development. The support should also be aligned with the country’s own priorities, policies and strategies for addressing climate change. This alignment ensures that the support is relevant and effective in achieving long-term goals.

## 183

There are several ways through which project preparation grants e.g., Green Climate Fund (GCF) Readiness and Preparatory Support, can be utilized to enhance the capacity of developing countries to access GCF resources. These include:

- **Capacity Building**: Enhancing the capacity of national designated authorities (NDAs), accredited entities and other relevant institutions.
- **Policy Development**: Developing policies and strategies that align with GCF requirements.
- **Institutional Strengthening**: Strengthening institutional arrangements for effective implementation of climate change actions.

## 184

It is mentioned above, country ownership is promoted as critical to the sustainability of the project. Similarly, the alignment of the project with national priorities is also emphasized. The project should be aligned with the national development strategies and other relevant national policies.

## 185

The project will include a wide range of stakeholders over large areas, therefore multi-level systems, including local governments, community-based organizations (CBOs), and civil society organizations (CSOs) are essential for the project’s success. The project will also be designed to ensure that it is inclusive and equitable.

## 186

The Green Climate Fund Readiness Preparation (RPM) has been designed for the project and is described in the EDRR. The RPM will support the government of Nepal to enhance its capacity to access GCF resources, including the preparation of a GCF Readiness Preparation Proposal (RPP). This will include the development of a National Designated Authority (NDA), an Accreditation Body (AB) and an accreditation process for national entities.

## G.2. Gender mainstreaming and action plan

### 187

Gender equality and social inclusion (GESI) assessment is one of the requirements of the GCF’s Gender Policy. The gender policy requires that all projects funded by the GCF must be designed to ensure that they are gender responsive, socially inclusive and environmentally sound.

### 188

The gender assessment includes the extent to which gender equality exists in Nepal. The country has a constitutional mandate for gender equality and social inclusion. The government of Nepal has also developed a Gender Equality Action Plan (GEAP) to address issues related to gender equality and social inclusion. The GEAP is designed to ensure that all projects funded by the GCF are gender responsive.

### 189

The project will be implemented in the Terai region of Nepal, which includes the districts of Bara, Parsa, Rautahat, Sarlahi, Mahottari and Dhanusha. The project area is characterized by a flat terrain with low elevation and fertile alluvial soil. The region has a tropical monsoon climate with high temperatures and heavy rainfall during the monsoon season.

### 190

The project will be implemented in collaboration with local governments, community-based organizations (CBOs) and civil society organizations (CSOs). These organizations will play an important role in ensuring that the project is inclusive and equitable.

# GREEN CLIMATE FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3 / PAGE #7

## GREEN CLIMATE FUND

### Introduction

The Green Climate Fund (GCF) was established to promote a paradigm shift in international climate change cooperation by supporting projects, programmes, policies and institutional capacity-building in developing countries.

### UNDP's Role in GCF

UNDP has been implementing the Green Climate Fund (GCF) since 2015. The GCF Readiness and Preparatory Support Programme (RPSP) is a key component of UNDP’s engagement with the GCF. The RPSP aims to enhance the capacity of developing countries to access GCF resources.

### H.1. Mandatory annexes

# Secretariat's assessment of FP272

**Proposal title:** Protecting biodiversity and assets at the free Global Lake Outdoors Club (GLOC) through integrated management in global river basins of Nepal.

**Accredited entity:** United Nations Development Programme (UNDP)

**Country:** Nepal

**Project status:** Small

## I. Overall assessment of the Secretariat

### Strengths
- The project focuses on assets at six Global Lake Outdoors Club (GLOC) sites.
- It aims to protect biodiversity and promote sustainable use in global river basins of Nepal, presenting innovative and long-term solutions for integrated management.
- The project builds on existing commitments and actions of communities in the global river basins of Nepal.
- It is aligned with the objectives of the Integrated Water Resources Management (IWRM) strategy related to the monitoring tasks.
- The project will be implemented through a participatory approach, including local authorities, non-governmental organizations, and community-based organizations.
- It will contribute towards ensuring that the project is consistent with the objectives of the Global Environment Facility (GEF) and that valuable information regarding biodiversity and ecosystem services is shared with relevant stakeholders.

### Points of concern
- The project does not fully adhere to the Drivers of Implementation Modality in integrated management. Hence, the Secretariat recommends that the project be reclassified as a Small Global Environment Facility (GEF) project.
- The Secretariat notes that the project will be implemented through a participatory approach and that local authorities are involved in order to ensure all stakeholders are included in the success of the project.

The project focuses on protecting biodiversity and assets at six Global Lake Outdoors Club sites. It aims to promote sustainable use in global river basins of Nepal, presenting innovative and long-term solutions for integrated management. The project builds on existing commitments and actions of communities in the global river basins of Nepal. It is aligned with the objectives of the Integrated Water Resources Management (IWRM) strategy related to the monitoring tasks. The project will be implemented through a participatory approach, including local authorities, non-governmental organizations, and community-based organizations. It will contribute towards ensuring that the project is consistent with the objectives of the Global Environment Facility (GEF) and that valuable information regarding biodiversity and ecosystem services is shared with relevant stakeholders.


---
**Page 108**
---

I will also pay special attention to private assets, transport networks, agricultural production, power supply from hydropower plants and other critical infrastructure in the target river basins, based on existing risk assessments.

AE-GLOF risk reductions in Nepal: This will include the development of a national AE-GLOF risk management plan and expand the coverage of GLOF risk assessments and early warning systems that are currently in place. It will also reduce the exposure of vulnerable communities to potential impacts through improved risk communication approaches, including a consolidated approach to addressing the barriers and challenges that hinder the implementation of AE-GLOF risk management measures. This includes promoting a comprehensive approach to AE-GLOF risk management in Nepal by promoting a multi-sectoral approach that integrates technical capabilities, physical protection measures, community-based disaster risk management (CBDRM) approaches, and hazard monitoring and EWSs. In addition, the project’s support to national institutions is commensurate because it will build on existing capacities and structures of relevant ministries and agencies at the local government level.

The three key risks worth considering in this funding proposal are consistent with the overall risk management approach and objectives of the GEF-6 IW-LDP. The proposed activities do not aim to address all risks but rather focus on the most significant ones.

## II. Summary of the Secretariat’s assessment

### 2.1 Project background

The project, “Transforming livelihoods and reducing risk from Glacial Lake Outburst Flood (GLOF) and climate change impacts in Bhotekoshi in glacial river basins in Nepal”, aims to substantially reduce GLOF risks and enhance climate resilience of communities living in the Bhotekoshi River basin. The project will be implemented by following GEF’s risk through an integrated approach encompassing physical lake outburst flood risk management, community-based disaster risk management (CBDRM), and livelihood support. It also aims to integrate water resources management approaches to develop climate-resilient livelihoods of communities living in the Bhotekoshi River basin. The project will be located in two target river basins, namely the Gandaki Basin and Koshi Basin, by following an integrated approach.

# Green Climate Fund

## Project Title: The Global Data Risk Framework

### Objective
The project aims to develop a comprehensive framework for managing data risk and ensuring data integrity across various sectors. This will involve creating guidelines, tools, and methodologies that can be applied globally.

### Background
Within the Green Climate Fund, the project targets the Thematic Global Data Risk Framework and Management Network which is in its formative stages. The project seeks to develop a robust framework for managing data risk and ensuring data integrity by providing guidance on best practices, standards, and methodologies. This will include developing tools and resources that can be used by various stakeholders within the GCF and beyond.

### Project Approach
The project will overcome these challenges by implementing an integrated approach to data management, including the development of a comprehensive framework for data risk management. This will involve:

- Developing guidelines and technical capacities for GCF risk management and mitigation.
- Enhancing existing tools and methodologies for data integrity and risk assessment.
- Strengthening internal monitoring and evaluation systems.

### Component 1: Development of the Global Data Risk Framework
The objective of this component is to develop a comprehensive framework that includes guidelines, standards, and best practices for managing data risk. This will involve:

- Developing a set of principles and standards for data management within the GCF.
- Creating tools and resources that can be used by various stakeholders to manage data risk effectively.

### Component 2: Capacity Building
The objective of this component is to build capacity among GCF staff and stakeholders in managing data risk. This will involve:

- Providing training and technical assistance to GCF staff and stakeholders.
- Developing guidelines and best practices for managing data risk.
- Enhancing the capacity of GCF to monitor and evaluate data integrity.

### Component 3: Monitoring and Evaluation
The objective of this component is to develop a monitoring and evaluation framework that can be used to assess the effectiveness of the Global Data Risk Framework. This will involve:

- Developing indicators and metrics for measuring data risk.
- Establishing a system for monitoring and evaluating the performance of the framework.

### Component 4: Stakeholder Engagement
The objective of this component is to engage with stakeholders in the development and implementation of the Global Data Risk Framework. This will involve:

- Conducting consultations and workshops with stakeholders.
- Developing guidelines and best practices for stakeholder engagement.
- Enhancing the capacity of GCF to engage with stakeholders effectively.

### Component 5: Communication and Knowledge Management
The objective of this component is to develop a communication and knowledge management strategy that can be used to promote the Global Data Risk Framework. This will involve:

- Developing guidelines and best practices for communication and knowledge management.
- Creating tools and resources that can be used by various stakeholders to promote the framework.
- Enhancing the capacity of GCF to communicate effectively with stakeholders.

### Objective 2.1: Strengthen capacity to implement enhanced ex-ante and ex-post safeguards

Objective 2.1 will focus on strengthening capacity to implement enhanced ex-ante and ex-post safeguards, including the development of guidance and Monitoring, Reporting, and Verification (MRV) systems to detect and monitor Green Climate Fund (GCF)-related risks and impacts.

Objective 2.2 will focus on strengthening risk management, including the implementation of enhanced risk reduction measures in priority geographic regions and sectors. # Green Climate Fund

## Project Title

### Project Summary

The project promotes an approach that fosters the development of an integrated and sustainable landscape management system in the target area. It aims to reduce deforestation and forest degradation, enhance carbon stocks, and improve livelihoods. The project will work with local communities to design and implement activities that address the drivers of deforestation and forest degradation. It will also support the development of a monitoring system to track progress towards these goals.

### Sustainable Development Potential

The project has significant potential to contribute to several sustainable development goals (SDGs), including:

- **SDG 15**: Life on Land
- **SDG 13**: Climate Action
- **SDG 8**: Decent Work and Economic Growth
- **SDG 7**: Affordable and Clean Energy

The project contributes directly to SDG 15 by reducing deforestation, enhancing forest carbon stocks, and improving the livelihoods of local communities. It also supports SDG 13 by addressing climate change through reduced emissions from deforestation and forest degradation. Additionally, the project promotes economic growth (SDG 8) by creating jobs and improving incomes for local communities. Finally, it supports SDG 7 by promoting the use of renewable energy sources.

### Needs of the Recipient

The project targets poor and near-poor farmers and watershed communities that continue to rely on unsustainable practices due to limited access to resources and support services. These communities are particularly vulnerable to the impacts of climate change and need assistance to adapt and mitigate their environmental impact.

### Sustainable Development Potential (continued)

The sustainable development potential of this investment is therefore estimated to be significant, with contributions across multiple SDGs.

# IV. Assessment of consistency with GCP safeguards and policies

## 4.1 Environmental and social safeguards

### c. Project overview

The project aims to subdue the lives and livelihoods of people and communities dependent on water-based areas. In September, the project will adopt an integrated approach from the following:

- **Environmental and Social Impact Assessment (ESIA)**: The project will be conducted through standard engineering methods, implementing disaster mitigation strategies such as flood control, drainage systems, and environmental management.
- **Reforestation**: Afforestation activities will be implemented to enhance biodiversity, increase forest cover, and improve water quality. The project will also include reforestation activities in degraded areas.
- **Community engagement**: The project will engage local communities through consultations and participatory planning processes to ensure that the project benefits are shared equitably among all stakeholders.

### d. Environmental and social risk category and safeguarded instrument

The project has been classified as Category B according to the IFC Performance Standards. This classification is aligned with the applicable level of the AE's Environmental and Social Management Plan (ESMP). The project will be implemented in accordance with the following standards:

- **IFC Performance Standard 1**: Assessment and management of environmental and social risks and impacts.
- **IFC Performance Standard 2**: Labor and working conditions.
- **IFC Performance Standard 3**: Resource efficiency and pollution prevention.

### e. Compliance with IFC Performance Standards

The following measures describe how the project responds to the standards:

- **ESIA Assessment and management of environmental and social risks and impacts**: The project will be conducted in accordance with the IFC Performance Standards.
- **Labor and working conditions**: The project will ensure that all workers are treated fairly and provided with safe working conditions.
- **Resource efficiency and pollution prevention**: The project will implement measures to minimize resource use and reduce pollution.

# Greenpeace Report

## Section I: Overview of Regulations and Compliance

The report will provide an overview of the regulations and requirements that will be in line with international standards and regulations, and UNEP and GEF policies and standards.

### a) ESS 1: Labour and working conditions

This aspect will promote respect and ensure the protection of human rights. The employment of private security forces should be the responsibility of the company and not subcontracted to third parties. The use of child labour is strictly prohibited, and the company must ensure that its suppliers adhere to these principles. The company will also ensure that its employees are treated with dignity and respect, regardless of their age or gender.

### b) ESS 2: Health and safety

The company will promote the health and safety of workers by ensuring safe working conditions. This includes providing adequate training and protective equipment for all workers. The following engineering measures have been implemented to ensure worker safety:

- Regular inspection and maintenance of machinery
- Proper ventilation and lighting in work areas
- Safe handling and storage of materials

The company also provides regular health check-ups and encourages employees to report any safety concerns.

### c) ESS 3: Resource efficiency and pollution prevention

Construction work related to the project will be carried out with a view to minimizing environmental impacts. This will include:

- Use of recycled materials
- Minimization of waste generation
- Implementation of waste management plans

The company will also promote the use of renewable energy sources and energy-efficient equipment.

## Section II: Environmental Health, Safety, and Security

### a) ESS 4: Community health, safety, and security

The company is committed to the safe flow of materials through its supply chain and other subcontractors for reducing GEF and Bank Board fiduciary risks. This includes:

- Implementation of environmental management systems
- Regular monitoring and reporting of environmental performance indicators
- Compliance with local laws and regulations

### b) ESS 5: Biodiversity protection measures

The company will implement biodiversity protection measures, including the development of biodiversity action plans. These plans will include:

- Identification of key biodiversity areas
- Development of management strategies for these areas
- Regular monitoring and reporting on biodiversity impacts

### c) ESS 6: Cultural heritage protection

The company is committed to protecting cultural heritage sites and ensuring that any construction or operation does not adversely affect these sites. This includes:

- Conducting cultural impact assessments
- Developing mitigation measures to protect cultural heritage sites
- Regular monitoring and reporting on the effectiveness of these measures

### d) ESS 7: Stakeholder engagement

The company will engage with stakeholders throughout the project lifecycle, including local communities, indigenous peoples, and other interested parties. This includes:

- Conducting regular consultations with stakeholders
- Providing information on project activities and impacts
- Incorporating stakeholder feedback into project planning and implementation

### e) ESS 8: Emergency preparedness and response

The company will implement emergency preparedness and response measures to ensure the safety of workers, communities, and the environment. This includes:

- Development of emergency response plans
- Regular training and drills for employees
- Establishment of emergency communication systems

### f) ESS 9: Community health and safety

The company is committed to promoting the health and safety of local communities. This includes:

- Provision of healthcare services to local communities
- Implementation of public health programs
- Collaboration with local health authorities to improve community health outcomes

### g) ESS 10: Security measures

The company will implement security measures to protect workers, assets, and the environment. This includes:

- Development of security plans
- Regular training for security personnel
- Implementation of access control measures

### h) ESS 11: grievance mechanisms

The company will establish grievance mechanisms to address any complaints or concerns raised by stakeholders. This includes:

- Provision of a confidential reporting system
- Timely investigation and resolution of grievances
- Regular monitoring and reporting on the effectiveness of the grievance mechanism

### i) ESS 12: Supply chain responsibility

The company will promote responsible business practices throughout its supply chain. This includes:

- Development of supplier codes of conduct
- Regular monitoring and evaluation of suppliers' performance
- Provision of training and capacity-building support to suppliers

## GCF-014/ARP/13

### Project Title: ESMF Implementation

#### a. ESS 7: Land acquisition and involuntary resettlement
The All Dons are foreseen to receive any compensation or benefits from the project activities. The project will not involve land acquisition, but it is expected that some communities may be affected by the project activities. The project will ensure that all communities affected by the project activities receive adequate compensation, often with the support of voluntary organizations and local governments. The project will also ensure that the involuntary resettlement is carried out in accordance with the principles of involuntary resettlement as outlined in the World Bank Guidelines on Involuntary Resettlement. The project will provide adequate compensation to all communities affected by the project activities, including those who are not directly affected.

#### b. ESS 7: Biodiversity conservation and sustainable management of living natural resources
The project will be implemented in areas where biodiversity is at risk due to human activities such as deforestation, agriculture, and mining. The project will aim to conserve biodiversity by protecting critical habitats and reducing the impact of human activities on these areas. The project will also promote sustainable use of living natural resources by implementing measures that reduce the pressure on these resources. The project will be implemented in accordance with the principles of the Convention on Biological Diversity (CBD) and the World Bank's Environmental and Social Safeguards.

#### c. GCF Indigenous Peoples Policy and ESS 7: Indigenous Peoples
The project meets the requirements of the GCF Indigenous Peoples Policy and ESS 7: Indigenous Peoples. The project will be implemented in accordance with the principles of free, prior and informed consent (FPIC) as outlined in the ILO Convention 169 on Indigenous and Tribal Peoples. The project will also promote the rights of indigenous peoples to participate in decision-making processes related to the project activities.

# Implementation arrangements

UNEP, as the AE, will provide a oversight role in the implementation of the EMP. The implementation arrangements will be developed through a consultative process with all relevant stakeholders and partners. The proposed implementing parties will establish a project steering committee to include leaders from key implementing agencies including UNEP, convention secretariats, regional organizations, nongovernmental organizations, academia, development partners, and other relevant groups.

The project steering committee will oversee the implementation of the EMP, ensure effective coordination among all stakeholders, and provide strategic guidance. The committee will also facilitate the establishment of a technical working group to support the implementation process. This working group will be composed of experts from various disciplines and will provide technical advice and support to the project steering committee.

## Stakeholder engagement

Stakeholders play an important part in the successful implementation of the EMP. Effective stakeholder engagement is essential for ensuring that the project meets its objectives and delivers benefits to all stakeholders. The project will engage with a wide range of stakeholders, including local communities, indigenous peoples, government representatives, private sector actors, and other relevant groups.

The project will also provide opportunities for meaningful access to information or participation in decision-making processes related to the implementation of the EMP. This includes providing opportunities for input from local communities, indigenous peoples, and other vulnerable groups (e.g., women, youth, persons with disabilities) on issues such as project design and implementation plans.

The project will also engage with relevant international organizations, including UNEP, UNDP, and other relevant agencies, to ensure effective coordination and collaboration. The project will also engage with relevant national and regional organizations, including government agencies, nongovernmental organizations, and academic institutions, to ensure effective implementation of the EMP.

## Greenwashing redress mechanism

A legal greenwashing redress mechanism will be established to address any instances of greenwashing that may occur during the implementation of the EMP. This mechanism will provide a means for stakeholders to report suspected cases of greenwashing and for the project steering committee to investigate and take appropriate action.

The project will also establish a grievance redress mechanism to address any complaints or concerns raised by stakeholders during the implementation process. This mechanism will be designed to ensure that all stakeholders have access to a fair and transparent process for resolving disputes and addressing grievances.

The project will also engage with relevant national and international organizations, including UNEP, UNDP, and other relevant agencies, to ensure effective coordination and collaboration in the establishment of the greenwashing redress mechanism. The project will also engage with relevant national and regional organizations, including government agencies, nongovernmental organizations, and academic institutions, to ensure effective implementation of the EMP.

- Ensuring that suppliers and contractors comply with the principles of gender equality in their own operations.

- Encouraging the use of products and services that support gender equality.

- Ensuring that investments are made in a manner that is consistent with the principles of gender equality.

- Promoting the use of data and evidence to inform decision-making related to gender equality.

## 4.3 Risks

### 4.3.1 Overall project assessment (medium risk)

The overall project is assessed as medium risk due to the country’s governance challenges that will impact the project’s ability to achieve its objectives. The project is also subject to risks associated with climate change, including sea-level rise and increased frequency of extreme weather events.

### 4.3.2 Accreditation equity/recycling entity capacity to execute the project (medium risk)

The accreditation entity has limited experience in implementing projects and technical expertise in DRM. The funding strategy is expected to increase from lessons learned in earlier GCF projects.

The accrediting entity, the Government of Nepal through its Ministry of Finance and Development Cooperation, will be responsible for the implementation of the project. The government’s capacity to implement the project is limited by its lack of experience in implementing such projects. The government’s capacity to implement the project is also limited by its lack of technical expertise in DRM.

The accrediting entity has limited experience in implementing projects and technical expertise in DRM. The funding strategy is expected to increase from lessons learned in earlier GCF projects.

# 4.4 Futility

## c.

Following a comprehensive review of the funding proposal for the Nepal GLOF risk reduction and rehabilitation project, it is recommended that the Board consider the following factors in its decision:

First, the need to focus on an integrated approach to disaster risk management, which includes the development of appropriate programming, transparent and inclusive governance arrangements, and the engagement of local governments, non-governmental organizations, and other stakeholders. This approach is consistent with the principles of the Sendai Framework for Disaster Risk Reduction 2015-2030.

Second, the differentiation of responsibilities between the Rt Hon and executing agencies is important to ensure effective implementation of the project. The Rt Hon should focus on policy development, coordination, advocacy, and oversight, while the executing agency should be responsible for day-to-day management and implementation. Additionally, the inclusion of appropriate risk management measures in the project design is essential.

Third, the need to consider the potential risks associated with the project, including those related to land tenure, land use planning, and environmental impacts. The Rt Hon should work closely with the executing agency to ensure that these risks are properly managed and mitigated.

Finally, the importance of monitoring and evaluation in ensuring the effectiveness of the project. The Rt Hon should establish a robust monitoring and evaluation system that includes regular reporting on progress, as well as an independent evaluation at the end of the project.

## d.

The Board is also requested to consider the following factors:

- The potential for the project to contribute to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs), particularly SDG 11 (sustainable cities and communities) and SDG 13 (climate action).
- The need to ensure that the project is aligned with the priorities of the Government of Nepal and other relevant stakeholders.
- The importance of engaging with local communities and other stakeholders in the design, implementation, and monitoring of the project.
- The potential for the project to be used as a model for similar projects in other countries.

The Board is also requested to consider the recommendations of the independent evaluation team that conducted an assessment of the project in 2019. The report of this assessment is available on the GCP website.

# 4.6 Legal assessment

An additional Standard Agreement was signed with the Accountable Entity on 1 August 2018, and became effective on 23 November 2018, which was amended and restated pertinent clauses in the Standard Agreement of 16 March 2016, and which became effective on 4 May 2017 (the “AAA”).

a. The Accountable Entity has provided a legal opinion/opinions confirming that it is lawfully empowered to enter into the proposed project agreement.

b. The proposed project will be implemented in Nepal, a country in which GEF is not currently engaged in any projects. The GEF Secretariat has therefore not previously sought approval for flagrant or egregious violations of the country’s laws and regulations. However, the GEF Secretariat has consulted with the relevant authorities in the countries that may be funded due to the absence of grievances and incentives for relevant GEF.

c. Therefore, it is recommended that disbursements by the GEF are subject to the GEF’s outlined safeguard procedures against flagrant and egregious violations of the country’s laws and regulations.

d. To address the situation cited in this section, it is recommended that any approval by the GEF Council be subject to the following condition:

e. Signature of the standard activity agreement in a form and substance satisfactory to the GEF Secretariat.

f. Compliance of the legal due diligence to the satisfaction of the GEF Secretariat.

# Independent Technical Advisory Panel’s assessment of FP272

**Proposal name:**
Preventing livelihoods and assets at risk from Global Lake Dusture Fund (GLDF) and climate change related flooding in Nepal.

**Accepted entity:**
United Nations Development Programme (UNDP)

**Country:**
Nepal

**Project size:**
Small

## L. Assessment of the Independent Technical Advisory Panel

### 1.1 Overview

The project entitled “Preventing livelihoods and assets at risk from Global Lake Dusture Fund (GLDF) and climate change related flooding in Nepal” aims to reduce the vulnerability of people living along high altitude ecosystems around the lake, Phewa, in Pokhara, Nepal. The project will be implemented through a combination of activities including: (i) capacity building for GLDF and climate change adaptation; (ii) improved livelihoods and assets protection from flooding; and (iii) enhanced institutional capacities to manage climate risks.

The project’s main objectives are:

- To integrate climate risk management into planning and development that takes climate risk and vulnerability into account.
- To contribute to the conservation of global lakes as a means to reduce GLDF and flood risks, and to increase preparedness to respond to natural hazards like floods.
- To enhance institutional capacities for climate risk informed development planning and disaster risk reduction.
- To enhance livelihoods and assets protection from flooding through integrated approaches including expanded hydrological information in GLDF areas based on improved data collection and analysis.
- To contribute to global lake monitoring, vegetation, geomorphology and rehabilitation of degraded ecosystems.

The project is integrated into environmental and social risk management strategies. Deforestation and land degradation are addressed through integrated approaches that include reforestation and sustainable agriculture practices. The project will also support the development of climate change adaptation plans for other lakes in Nepal.

This project is the second in UNDP’s GLDF 144 countries of which UNDP 36 is a country to be supported from GLDF as a grant. The remaining USD 13.79 million will be co-financed by national

# Green Climate Fund

## Project Title: Enhancing Resilience through Integrated Landscape Management

### Introduction
Initiatives and private-sector stakeholders through in-situ contributions, eco-sholing, and embedded expertise on monitoring and maintenance commitments.

### Project Description
The project aims to enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities by integrating landscape management (LM) with the principles of Ecosystem-based Adaptation (EbA). EbA is a holistic approach that combines the principles of biodiversity conservation and sustainable development. The project will focus on restoring degraded ecosystems, promoting ecosystem services, and enhancing community resilience through integrated LM.

### Impact Potential
**Scale: Medium-High**

1. **Beneficiaries**
   - The project is expected to benefit 385,506 people (57% women and 43% men), with representation agreements covering 1.3% of the population in Nepal.
   - The project will improve the livelihoods of at least 20,000 households by providing improved ecosystem services and reduced exposure to natural disasters.

2. **Economic Benefits**
   - The project benefits are primarily concentrated in the Terai region, where poverty rates are high (45% of the population lives below the national poverty line).
   - The project will generate employment opportunities for local communities through various activities such as planting trees and maintaining ecosystems.
   - The project aims to create sustainable livelihoods by promoting agroforestry, ecotourism, and other ecosystem-based enterprises.

3. **Environmental Benefits**
   - To avoid maladaptation, the project applies a hybrid approach including integrated LM with EbA principles.
   - Vegetation restoration will reduce soil erosion and improve water retention.
   - Integrated interventions include hard engineering (e.g., check dams) and soft engineering (e.g., afforestation).
   - The project aims to restore 50,000 hectares of degraded land through sustainable land management practices.

4. **Social Benefits**
   - The project will enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities by providing access to ecosystem services.
   - The project will support the development of local institutions and community-based organizations (CBOs) for long-term sustainability.
   - The project aims to improve the livelihoods of at least 10,000 households through sustainable land management practices.

### Conclusion
The project is a critical component in the climate adaptation agenda of Nepal. By integrating EbA principles with LM, the project aligns with the National Adaptation Plan (NAP) and contributes to achieving the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs).

# Green Climate Fund

serves developing countries, small island states, and least developed countries by providing grants and climate finance.

## Project overview

The GCF aims to promote a paradigm shift in development investments to support and catalyze a transformation towards a climate-resilient and low-emission society. The ability to increase GCF risk tolerance is crucial to achieving this goal. The GCF has adopted a risk-based approach that includes the following:

- **Risk appetite:** The GCF's risk appetite is defined by its willingness to accept certain levels of risk in order to achieve its objectives.
- **Risk tolerance:** The GCF's risk tolerance is the degree of risk that the GCF is willing to accept before taking action to mitigate or avoid further risk.

### Risk management

The GCF has established a comprehensive risk management framework that includes the following components:

- **Risk identification:** The GCF identifies risks through its project pipeline, due diligence processes, and ongoing monitoring.
- **Risk assessment:** The GCF assesses risks based on their likelihood and impact.
- **Risk mitigation:** The GCF implements measures to reduce or eliminate identified risks.

### Risk appetite

The GCF's risk appetite is outlined in the following table:

| Risk Category | Risk Appetite |
|---------------|---------------|
| Financial Risks | Moderate |
| Operational Risks | Low |
| Reputational Risks | Very Low |

## Country oversight

The GCF project to Nepal is strongly aligned with the country's climate change priorities and objectives. The project aims to support the implementation of the Nationally Determined Contributions (NDCs) and the National Adaptation Plan (NAP). The project will contribute to the achievement of the following outcomes:

- **Increased resilience:** The project will enhance the resilience of vulnerable communities to climate change impacts.
- **Improved livelihoods:** The project will support the development of sustainable livelihoods for poor and marginalized communities.

### Project components

The project includes the following components:

- **Disaster Risk Reduction (DRR):** The project will implement DRR measures, including early warning systems, community-based disaster risk management (CBDRM), and climate-resilient infrastructure.
- **Climate-Smart Agriculture:** The project will promote climate-smart agriculture practices, including sustainable farming methods and improved crop varieties.

### Project implementation

The project will be implemented by the Government of Nepal through its relevant ministries. The project will be coordinated with other ongoing climate change initiatives in the country to ensure a coherent approach to climate change adaptation and mitigation.

## Scale: High

The GCF project to Nepal is expected to have a significant impact on the country's climate change priorities. The project will support the implementation of the NDCs and the NAP, contributing to increased resilience and improved livelihoods for vulnerable communities.

# Green IT Code

## Efficiency and effectiveness

### a.
The project requires a total of about 1.5 million in GEF grant funding, co-financed by the government of Nigeria. The project will be implemented over five years with an initial implementation period of two years.

### b.
The project demonstrates strong economic viability under most scenarios. The net present value (NPV) is USD 3.4 million and the internal rate of return (IRR) is 15%. The project has a payback period of less than three years, which is considered short by industry standards for such investments.

### c.
The co-financing ratio is relatively modest at 1:0.8, with respect to co-financing from the government of Nigeria. Although the government's commitment fell over GEF-3 cycle, only USD 4 million is pledged for this project. The project has a high level of private capital invested and mostly financing agreements are in place.

# The project builds on best practices from previous GLOF investigation efforts in Nepal and Bhutan, including the RIEB pilot project. It integrates hydrological modeling, remote sensing, and field investigations to improve the understanding of glacierized basins, particularly in ENP. Benefits and constraints of disaster response mechanisms are also considered. The project will enhance the capacity of local institutions to manage GLOF risks by providing training on risk assessment, hazard mapping, and early warning systems. It will also support the development of regional and national policies for disaster risk reduction.

## II. Overall remarks from the Independent Technical Advisory Panel

### c.
The GLOF project is aligned with country's climate adaptation priorities and contributes to the achievement of the Sendai Framework targets on disaster risk reduction. The project aligns well with the government's policy on integrated water resources management, including high-altitude glacial lakes, and supports the development of a national strategy for disaster risk reduction. It also aligns with the government's commitment to climate change adaptation and mitigation.

### d.
To effectively monitor and establish the GLOF mechanisms:

- Clearly define the objectives, deliverables, and target groups.
- Instruments and approaches should be aligned with the project's goals.

### e.
Integrate local and indigenous knowledge, consider a comprehensive monitoring strategy that includes both traditional and modern methods. This approach recognizes specific measures at the local level, facilitates products, and leads to better understanding of the risks and vulnerabilities.

### f.
Ensure the team builds capacity and expertise in hydrological modeling and GLOF risk assessment. This includes training on remote sensing resources and disaster response mechanisms. The project should also consider the integration of climate change adaptation (CCA) and disaster risk reduction (DRR) approaches, including studies on climate change impacts and vulnerability assessments.

## Proposal title

Protecting biodiversity and assets at the first Global Biodiversity Fund (GBF) and climate change mainstreaming in the United Nations Development Programme (UNDP).

### Accredited entity:

United Nations Development Programme (UNDP)

### Country:

Nepal

### Project size:

Small

## Impact potential

The Medium-High rating is noted.

Installed capacity of hydropower for isolated communities will be supported through long-term policy and regulatory frameworks. The project will support the development of a climate change mainstreaming strategy and action plan, which will enhance the resilience of biodiversity assets to climate change. The project will also provide technical assistance to local governments to develop and implement biodiversity management plans. This will contribute to the conservation of key biodiversity areas and improve the livelihoods of communities that depend on these ecosystems.

## Paradigm shift potential

The Medium-High rating is noted.

While UNDP has the partners of a fully owned national application plan, the project establishes a new paradigm for climate change mainstreaming in the UNDP. The GBF will promote the enhanced systems and technologies PPP partnership, addressing climate change adaptation and mitigation through biodiversity conservation. The project will also support the development of a community-based biodiversity database that can be considered useful to the long-term sustainability of biodiversity assets. This will help to ensure that the project is key component of the UNDP framework that will be developed at project completion.

While other planning for demonstration initiatives is not a primary target of the project’s impact potential, it is expected that the project will contribute to the development and implementation of risk maps and risk information planning. In particular, the preparation and design of risk maps and risk information planning will be useful in demonstrating the effectiveness of the project. The project will also support the development of a community-based biodiversity database that can be considered useful to the long-term sustainability of biodiversity assets.

# Gender Equality, Disability and Social Inclusion (GEDSI) Action Plan

Protecting livelihoods and assets at risk from Glacial Lake Outburst Floods (GLOFs) and climate change-induced flooding in glacial river basins of Nepal

Seven-year project proposal submitted to Green Climate Fund

September 2021

## Acronyms

- **CBO**: Community Based Organization
- **DE**: International Development Enterprise
- **DDC**: District Development Committee
- **DOE**: Department of Energy
- **DPIDG**: Department of Physical Infrastructure and Transport Management
- **DRR**: Disaster Risk Reduction
- **DNPWC**: Department of National Parks and Wildlife Conservation
- **FMO**: Foreign Ministry of Nepal
- **GEDSI**: Gender Equality, Disability and Social Inclusion
- **GLF**: Glacial Lake Outburst Floods
- **GOF**: Global Environment Facility

# Project's expected results

- Increased resilience and enhanced livelihoods of the most vulnerable people, communities and regions.

## GEDSI outcome statements of the project

### Institutional level

- Climate Change induced disaster, water-related government institutions at federal, provincial and municipality and ward levels are strengthened.
- Improved climate change information services and early warning systems for climate smart agriculture, integrated watershed management, GLOF/DRR/CGC tools and methods, and in planning and monitoring, ward level planning cycle, and monitoring regularity.

### Community level

- Poor and marginalized women and men are leading and managing at least 30% of the GLOF/DRR, Integrated Watershed Management, and Ecosystem Based Adaptation (EBA) activities in the project areas.
- Improved climate change resilience and adaptation through improved livelihoods, change through improvement of their capacity and accessed early warning system for climate smart agriculture, value-based solutions, integrated watershed management, alternative energy technologies and agroforestry-based livelihood.

## Risks

- Costs of adopting or sharing new GLOF/DRR knowledge and technologies for women and marginalized groups - in terms of increased labor, time, language.
- Lack of capacity among targeted and partnering institutions (priority - budget, quality of training and inputs). GEDSI might be high priority.

*Government institutions: Ministry MuFE, MoEWH, DHM, DoFSC, DNPWC, local governments.*

# Protectorate visit

## Current GLOF risk assessment approaches

- GLOF risk assessment approaches are well established and widely used in very limited areas for GLOF.
- GLOF risk reduction methodologies are not GEDs compliant.

### i) Integrate GED into risk change scenarios

- Technical and scientific Local relevant data
- GEDs integrated into government's planning

## GEDs compliance monitoring

### i) Review at least 5 GEDs from different countries

- SOC (Safety of Dams) guidelines like GEDs are not very limited for GLOF.
- Ensure that at least one GED is reviewed by an independent expert and local authorities.

### ii) Ensure at least one GED is reviewed by an independent expert and local authorities.

## Indicator development

### i) Indicator at least 5 GEDs from different countries

- GEDs integrated into government's planning
- GEDs compliance monitoring guidelines are not very limited for GLOF.
- Ensure that at least one GED is reviewed by an independent expert and local authorities.

- Execute entity responsible for GEDs Expert and partners.

### ii) Year 4: 50%

- Execute entity responsible for GEDs Expert and partners.

### iii) Year 7: 80%

- Execute entity responsible for GEDs Expert and partners.

# Strategies for Legal Empowerment of Women in Kashmir Basin Area

| Strategy | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| 1. Establish a legal aid system with trained paralegals to provide free legal advice and representation for women facing domestic violence, sexual harassment, and other forms of gender-based violence. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |
| 2. Organize women's groups and provide training on their rights and how to access legal services. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |
| 3. Strengthen/establish mechanisms for legal literacy and awareness-raising campaigns in local communities, particularly among women. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |

# Vulnerable Women's Groups

| Strategy | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| 1. Establish a system and mechanism to identify and monitor women at risk in LRFs it aims at yearly basis. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |
| 2. Number of women supported by made available to promote women's rights in LRFs it aims at yearly basis. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |

# Independent Experts

| Strategy | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| 1. Year 1-2: Executing unity with local experts and community leaders in LRFs patterns. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |
| 2. Year 3-4: Executing unity with local experts and community leaders in LRFs patterns. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |

# Organizing Women's Groups

| Strategy | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| 1. Organize groups of poor, marginalized women and provide training on their rights and how to access legal services. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |
| 2. Women, poor, marginalized group women and provide training on their rights and how to access legal services. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |

# Organizing a 3-day Training on Climate Risk

| Strategy | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| 1. Organize a 3-day training on Climate Risk for women, with all expenses covered by the project, and encourage them to engage in climate risk mitigation and adaptation activities. | No covering and support from the government is required to implement this strategy which falls under the purview of the legal aid provision for the provision for empowerment for women's rights. |

| Strategy | Description |
|----------|-------------|

# Projects expected output 2: Improved hazard monitoring and the generation and dissemination of early warning to communities and implementers

## Project activities

| Activities | GEOBIA | GEOBIA indicators | Timeline | Responsible | Estimated budget (USD) |

# Other agencies in charge of women’s development

## Agriculture

- Highly marginalized women farmers and their families are supported through HIMAMANTS’ project.
- Women are trained to produce organic compost, vermicompost, bio-pesticides, etc., with the help of experts from the Agriculture University.
- Women are also trained in organic farming and marketing. They have formed groups for collective marketing.

## Highly marginalized women farmers

- 150 highly marginalized women farmers are supported through HIMAMANTS’ project.
- Women are trained to produce organic compost, vermicompost, bio-pesticides, etc., with the help of experts from the Agriculture University.
- Women are also trained in organic farming and marketing. They have formed groups for collective marketing.

## Convergence

- Convergence is done through convergence guidelines provided by the National Rural Employment Guarantee Act (NREGA).
- Women’s groups are linked to various government programs, including NREGA, MGNREGA, etc.
- Women’s groups are also linked to the district budget.

## Expert and training

- Expert and training support is provided for women’s groups by HIMAMANTS.
- Training is conducted on organic farming and marketing.
- Women’s groups are supported through various government schemes.
